<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every sufficiently large admissible Hassett discriminant, we prove that a very general cubic fourfold of that discriminant is irrational, although its Kuznetsov component is equivalent to the ordinary derived category of a projective K3 surface. The discriminant threshold is ineffective. This disproves Kuznetsov's rationality conjecture. The same cubics have associated untwisted polarized K3 surfaces in the Hodge-theoretic sense, so they also disprove the sufficiency direction of the associated-K3 rationality prediction.

- **claim-large-admissible-irrationality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is an integer $d_0$ such that, for every admissible $d>d_0$, a very general cubic fourfold $X$ in $\mathcal C_d$ is irrational and admits an exact $\mathbb C$-linear equivalence $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ku}}(X)\simeq D^b(\operatorname{Coh}S)$$ for a smooth projective K3 surface $S$.
  - Explanation: Above an ineffective common discriminant threshold, very general cubics in each admissible Hassett divisor are irrational despite having an ordinary K3 derived category as their Kuznetsov component.
- **claim-associated-k3-insufficiency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-associated-k3)): Let $d_0$ be the threshold in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For every admissible $d>d_0$, a very general $X\in\mathcal C_d$ is irrational and admits a labelling $K$ of discriminant $d$ and a primitively polarized smooth projective K3 surface $(S_{\mathrm{Hdg}},L)$, with $L^2=d$, for which there is an integral Hodge isometry $$K^\perp \simeq L^\perp(-1).$$ Here $L^\perp\subset H^2(S_{\mathrm{Hdg}},\mathbb Z)$, and the surface cup pairing is reversed. Thus an associated untwisted polarized K3 surface in this Hodge-theoretic sense is not sufficient for rationality.
  - Explanation: The irrational cubics also have an untwisted polarized K3 association with an integral Hodge isometry and reversed surface pairing. Such an association does not imply rationality.
- **claim-hilbert-square-insufficiency** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-hilbert-square)): Let $d_0$ be the threshold in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For every integer $n\ge2$ such that $d=2n^2+2n+2>d_0$, a very general $X\in\mathcal C_d$ is irrational and its Fano variety of lines $F(X)$ is birational to the Hilbert square $S_{\mathrm{Hilb}}^{[2]}$ of a smooth projective K3 surface $S_{\mathrm{Hilb}}$. There are infinitely many such discriminants. Thus birationality of $F(X)$ to a K3 Hilbert square is not sufficient for rationality of $X$.
  - Explanation: An infinite discriminant subfamily has irrational very general cubics whose varieties of lines are birational to K3 Hilbert squares.
- **claim-integral-chow-diagonal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-chow-diagonal)): Let $d_0$ be the threshold in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For every admissible $d>d_0$, a very general $X\in\mathcal C_d$ is irrational and has universally trivial $\operatorname{CH}_0$: for every field extension $F/\mathbb C$, the degree map $$\deg\colon\operatorname{CH}_0(X_F)\longrightarrow\mathbb Z$$ is an isomorphism. This universal $\operatorname{CH}_0$ property is equivalent to the existence of a point $x\in X(\mathbb C)$, a proper closed algebraic subset $D\subsetneq X$, and a codimension-four cycle $\Gamma$ with integer coefficients supported on $D\times X$ such that $$[\Delta_X]=[X\times\{x\}]+[\Gamma] \quad\text{in }\operatorname{CH}^4(X\times X)$$ with integral coefficients.
  - Explanation: The irrational very general cubics have universally trivial zero-cycles and an integral Chow decomposition of the diagonal. The resulting necessary condition for stable rationality does not decide stable rationality.
- **claim-mrc-fibration-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-mrc-fibration)): Let $V\dashrightarrow W$ be a dominant rational map whose geometric generic fibre is integral and has a rationally connected smooth proper model. Then $R(V)$ and $R(W)$ are birational, and hence $u(V)=u(W)$. In particular, for any vector bundle $\mathcal E$ of positive rank on an integral variety $W$, $$u(\mathbb P_W(\mathcal E))=u(W).$$ The test vanishes on rationally connected varieties and on varieties of dimension at most one. For an integral surface $W$, it is one precisely when $W$ is birational to a member of $\mathcal S$.
  - Explanation: A geometrically integral rationally connected generic fibre preserves the MRC base and its K3 test. This covers projective bundles and identifies the low-dimensional vanishing cases.
- **claim-small-orbit-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-small-orbits)): Suppose $k\simeq\mathbb C(x,y)$. A finite Galois orbit of size one or two has test value zero. More generally, if a finite extension $\ell/k$ has a nonidentity $\mathbb C$-automorphism, then $u(\ell)=0$.
  - Explanation: Small Galois orbits over the rational surface field are not counted. More generally, a nonidentity complex field automorphism prevents a finite extension from being the field of a test K3 surface.
- **claim-divisorial-cocycle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-cocycle)): The sums in <a href="#pre:cocycle-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:cocycle-definition">[pre:cocycle-definition]</a> are finite. For birational maps $f:X\dashrightarrow Y$ and $g:Y\dashrightarrow Z$ of normal projective varieties, $$\label{pre:cocycle-additivity} c_u(g\circ f)=c_u(f)+c_u(g),\qquad c_u(f^{-1})=-c_u(f).$$ An isomorphism in codimension one has value zero. If $\pi:\widetilde X\to X$ is the blow-up of a smooth projective variety along a smooth center with connected components $Z_i$ of codimension at least two, then $$\label{pre:blowup-sign} c_u(\pi^{-1})=\sum_i u(Z_i),\qquad c_u(\pi)=-\sum_i u(Z_i).$$
  - Explanation: The divisorial count is finite and additive, reverses sign under inversion, and evaluates on smooth blowups as the signed tests of the connected centers.
- **claim-smooth-weak-factorization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-pre-weak-factorization)): A birational map between smooth projective varieties over $\mathbb C$ factors into a finite zigzag of blow-ups and blow-downs along smooth connected centers, with all intermediate varieties smooth and projective. Nontrivial steps may be taken to have centers of codimension at least two.
  - Explanation: A smooth projective complex birational map admits a finite smooth projective factorization by blowups and blowdowns, with divisor-center isomorphisms omitted.
- **claim-transcendental-hodge-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gw-hodge)): The forms on $T_M$ and $T_Z$ are nondegenerate. The rational Hodge structure $T_{M,\mathbb Q}$ is generated by its $(3,1)$ and $(1,3)$ subspaces, and $T_{Z,\mathbb Q}$ is generated by its $(2,0)$ and $(0,2)$ subspaces. The only off-diagonal types in the even cohomology of $M$ are $(3,1)$ and $(1,3)$ in degree four. If $i:Z\hookrightarrow M$ is a surface embedding, then $$i^*T_{M,\mathbb Q}=0,\qquad i_*T_{Z,\mathbb Q}=0.$$
  - Explanation: The relevant transcendental pairings are nondegenerate and their Hodge structures are generated by the stated off-diagonal types. Restriction to and Gysin pushforward from a surface annihilate the corresponding transcendental inputs.
- **claim-integral-operator-blowup-splitting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gw-blowup)): Let $\pi:Y\to M$ be the blowup of a connected smooth center $Z$ of codimension $r\geq2$ in a rationally connected smooth projective fourfold. If $Z$ is a surface, the classical embeddings identify $$\label{gw:split-equation} (T_Y,q_Y)=(T_M,q_M)\perp(T_Z(-1),-q_Z) \quad\text{over }\mathbb Z, \qquad G_Y=G_M\oplus\mathbb Q\,\operatorname{id}_{T_Z}.$$ The second factor is omitted when $T_Z=0$. For a point or curve center, $T_Y=T_M$ integrally and $G_Y=G_M$.
  - Explanation: A surface blowup adds its entire shifted integral transcendental lattice and an independently acting scalar algebra factor. Point and curve centers add no transcendental contribution.
- **claim-local-projective-bundle-tensors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gw-local)): On any of the projective-bundle pieces just described, a connected genus-zero invariant, either absolute or relative to the indicated infinity boundary, with two variable insertions in the corresponding copies of $W$ and all other insertions rational and diagonal is a rational scalar multiple of $q_Z$ on the corresponding copies of $W$. It vanishes if the projected curve class on $Z$ is nonzero. These statements allow ordinary descendants and expanded targets.
  - Explanation: Local connected genus-zero tensors on the auxiliary projective bundles are scalar surface cup pairings, and vanish for positive projected curve class. Ordinary descendants and expansions remain allowed.
- **claim-oriented-tree-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gw-path)): In either normal-cone degeneration above, consider a genus-zero tree with exactly two variable insertions of Hodge differences $+2$ and $-2$. Every branch disjoint from the path joining them supplies a rational diagonal class at its attachment. Along that path the variable spaces are copies of $T_{M,\mathbb Q}$ or $W$, with the appropriate Tate shifts. Orient the path from the first labelled variable marking to the second, and order the two slots at each path vertex in that direction. Suppose that, with this slot order, each vertex tensor belongs to the applicable row of the following table. Then contraction of the path belongs to the row determined by its two remaining ends: $$\begin{array}{c\|c} \text{two end spaces}&\text{allowed tensors}\\ \hline T_{M,\mathbb Q},T_{M,\mathbb Q} & (u,v)\longmapsto q_M(Au,v),\quad A\in G_M,\\ W,W&\mathbb Qq_Z,\\ T_{M,\mathbb Q},W\text{ or }W,T_{M,\mathbb Q}&0. \end{array}$$ One may replace $G_M$ by any specified rational unital operator algebra, provided that each path vertex still satisfies its applicable row in this oriented slot order. In the reconstruction arguments below, this vertex hypothesis is supplied by the local-tensor lemma or by an earlier induction case.
  - Explanation: After diagonal side branches are pruned, the oriented path between the variable markings composes the allowed operator or scalar tensors. The conclusion requires the stated restriction at every path vertex.
- **claim-restricted-relative-reconstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gw-relative)): Every such tensor satisfies the three rows of Lemma <a href="#gw:path" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gw:path">14</a>.
  - Explanation: The relative tensors with ordinary insertions pulled back from the original fourfold satisfy the old, exceptional, and mixed tensor restrictions through simultaneous strong induction.
- **claim-divisor-relative-reconstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gw-divisor)): For $(Y,D)$, allow arbitrary ordinary diagonal insertions and arbitrary ordinary descendants. Allow the two variables either as ordinary classes in $T_M$ or $W\subset H^4(Y)$, or as boundary classes in a tautological copy of $W$ in $H^*(D)$. Then relative tensors with two $T_M$ ends belong to $\operatorname{pr}_M G_Y$, tensors with two $W$ ends are scalar cup pairings, and mixed tensors vanish.
  - Explanation: Allowing arbitrary ordinary diagonal insertions on the blowup still gives projected old operators, scalar exceptional pairings, and vanishing mixed tensors.
- **claim-whole-integral-block-cancellation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-gw-blocks)): Along a smooth weak factorization starting at $\mathbb P^4$, each transcendental lattice is an integral orthogonal sum of whole shifted surface-transcendental lattices. Its operator algebra is the product of independent rational scalar algebras on those summands. A blowdown with nonzero surface-transcendental contribution removes one whole summand, whose integral polarized Hodge-isometry class is that of the entire transcendental lattice of its center. A step with zero transcendental contribution leaves the decomposition unchanged.
  - Explanation: Independent scalar factors distinguish entire surface contributions throughout weak factorization. A blowdown removes a whole saturated integral polarized Hodge block, even when different blocks are Hodge-isomorphic.
- **claim-rank-twenty-one-k3-centers** (classification, [source](#stmt-gw-k3-center)): Let $Z$ be a smooth connected projective surface with $h^{2,0}(Z)=1$ and $\operatorname{rank}T_Z=21$. Then $Z$ is birational to a Picard-number-one K3 surface. Its primitive ample generator has square $\|\operatorname{disc}T_Z\|$. If this square is $d>2$ and $\operatorname{End}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(T_{Z,\mathbb Q})=\mathbb Q$, that K3 surface has trivial automorphism group.
  - Explanation: The surface rank and geometric-genus assumptions force a Picard-number-one K3 minimal model. Its polarization degree is the integral transcendental discriminant, and the further degree and endomorphism assumptions force trivial automorphisms.
- **claim-rationality-forces-net-k3-contribution** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-gw-net-center)): Let $M$ be a rationally connected smooth projective fourfold with $$h^{3,1}(M)=1,\qquad \operatorname{rank}T_M=21,\qquad \|\operatorname{disc}T_M\|=d>2,\qquad \operatorname{End}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(T_{M,\mathbb Q})=\mathbb Q.$$ Let $\mathcal S$ consist of the Picard-number-one K3 surfaces $S$ whose shifted integral transcendental lattice $(T_S(-1),-q_S)$ is Hodge-isometric to $(T_M,q_M)$, and use this collection in the test $u$ of the preliminary section. If $f:\mathbb P^4\dashrightarrow M$ is a birational map, then $$c_u(f)=1.$$ Every member of $\mathcal S$ has degree $d$ and trivial automorphism group.
  - Explanation: For a rational fourfold with the prescribed integral transcendental data, smooth factorization has a net test contribution of one. The selected K3 class need not be assumed nonempty beforehand.
- **claim-k3-pencil-genus-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-bd-pencil)): Let $S$ be a degree-$d$ K3 surface. If a dominant rational map from $S$ to a smooth projective curve has connected general fibre, that curve is $\mathbb P^1$. If $g$ is the genus of the smooth general fibre on a resolution of the map, then $$2g-2\geq\sqrt d.$$ For an arbitrary dominant rational map to a curve the same inequality holds for every geometric general fibre component, after normalization.
  - Explanation: Pencils on a Picard-number-one polarized K3 surface have general-fibre genus bounded below by the polarization degree. Stein factorization extends the statement to normalized geometric fibre components.
- **claim-bounded-mrc-k3-degree** (bound, [source](#stmt-bd-bounded-mrc)): For a birationally bounded collection of varieties of dimension at most three, there is an integer $b$ such that any degree-$d$ K3 surface birational to the MRC base of a member satisfies $d\leq b$.
  - Explanation: Birational boundedness in the stated dimensions bounds the polarization degrees of Picard-number-one K3 MRC bases, independently of the quotient maps.
- **claim-bounded-geometric-data-exclusions** (bound, [source](#stmt-bd-exclusions)): Fix the finite-type families occurring in each of the following statements, and fix an integer $N\geq1$. There is an integer $b$, depending only on these families and on $N$, with the following properties.* 1. *No member of a specified birationally bounded collection of varieties of dimension at most three has a degree-$d$ K3 MRC base with $d>b$.* 2. *Let $(Y,D)$ range over a bounded collection of projective curve or surface pairs. The smooth projective curves covering the curve members with degree at most $N$, *étale off $D$*, have bounded genus. No surface field obtained by such a cover of a surface member is the field of a degree-$d$ K3 surface for $d>b$.* 3. *Let $T\dashrightarrow C$ be a dominant map from an integral complex surface to a smooth projective curve. Suppose each normalized geometric generic fibre component is a degree-at-most-$N$ cover of a member of a fixed bounded collection of curve pairs, *étale off the marked boundary*. Then $T$ is not birational to a degree-$d$ K3 surface for $d>b$.* 4. *Let $T\dashrightarrow C$ be a dominant map from an integral complex threefold to a smooth projective curve. Suppose the geometric generic fibre components are uniruled and belong to a fixed birationally bounded collection of surfaces. Then the MRC base of $T$ is not a degree-$d$ K3 surface for $d>b$.* *The bounded geometric data in <span class="upright">(iii)</span> and <span class="upright">(iv)</span> are understood after algebraic closure of $\mathbb C(C)$; neither the total spaces $T$ nor the maps to $C$ are required to be bounded. The assertions apply to each field factor of a finite algebra separately.
  - Explanation: Fixed bounded varieties, marked covers, and geometric generic fibre families exclude large-degree K3 tests. Both cover degree and branch location are controlled; total spaces and maps need not themselves be bounded in the fibrewise cases.
- **claim-central-small-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-bd-central)): Consider a wall in this geography on a smooth projective start $(W,\Phi)$. Assume that $(W,\Phi)$ is $\epsilon$-lc. Then the relative small models in the link have a common set of prime valuations and admit a small modification $N^{\mathrm c}/Q$ such that $$-(K_{N^{\mathrm c}}+\Phi_{N^{\mathrm c}}) \quad\text{is nef and big over }Q.$$ Its log-anticanonical ample-model map $N^{\mathrm c}\to Z^{\mathrm c}$ is small. Both $N^{\mathrm c}$ and $Z^{\mathrm c}$ are $\epsilon$-lc with their transformed boundaries. All the link vertices and contraction bases are rational contractions of a small $\mathbb Q$-factorialization of $Z^{\mathrm c}$. If $\Phi=0$, all the small models can be taken terminal.
  - Explanation: At a geography wall, ample subtraction and the movable-cone argument produce a central small model and small ample-model contraction while preserving prime valuations and the discrepancy lower bound.
- **claim-higher-start-mark-preservation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bd-higher-start)): Let $(W,\Phi)$ be smooth projective with SNC boundary whose coefficients belong to $\{0,c\}$, where $0<c<1/4$ and $\dim W\leq3$. Suppose two Mori fibre spaces are outputs of negative $(K_W+\Phi)$ programs. On a higher smooth common start resolving the maps to both endpoints, keep the marks on original prime divisors and assign coefficient either $0$ or $c$ to any new prime divisor, with SNC total support. The same two outputs remain outcomes of the program for this new marked pair. In its geography the original marked discrepancy bound can be taken to be $\epsilon=1-c$, independently of the higher start. The analogous assertion holds for ordinary terminal Mori fibre spaces with zero boundary and $\epsilon=1$, including the four-dimensional paths used below.
  - Explanation: Higher smooth common starts preserve the given endpoints when original marks are retained and new marks obey the specified rule. The discrepancy bound remains independent of the resolution.
- **claim-cartier-family-stratification** (construction, [source](#stmt-bd-cartier-strata)): Let $\mathcal P$ be a bounded collection of marked projective klt log-Fano pairs $(Y,B)$, with coefficients of $B$ in a fixed finite subset of $\mathbb Q$. Finitely many stratified families, after finite base changes of their parameter spaces, can be chosen so that:* 1. *the log-canonical divisor is relatively $\mathbb Q$-Cartier, with a common Cartier multiple on each stratum;* 2. *there is a simultaneous log resolution of the marked support, and the coefficients of the crepant pullback of $B$ are constant on each stratum;* 3. *any further specified rational Weil divisor, with bounded marked support and coefficients in a fixed finite subset of $\mathbb Q$, which is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier for every member has a common Cartier multiple on each stratum, and its exceptional pullback coefficients are constant.* *The same assertions about $\mathbb Q$-Cartier classes hold for a bounded collection of varieties with rational singularities whose smooth projective resolutions have $H^1(\mathcal O)=H^2(\mathcal O)=0$. In these families $\mathbb Q$-factoriality is a constructible condition.
  - Explanation: Bounded marked log-Fano data admit finite stratifications and finite base changes with common Cartier multiples, simultaneous resolutions, and constant crepant coefficients. The stated vanishing assumptions also yield constructibility of factoriality.
- **claim-uniform-marked-contraction-diagrams** (bound, [source](#stmt-bd-contractions)): For a bounded collection of projective klt log-Fano pairs with fixed finite coefficient set, all diagrams obtained from a small $\mathbb Q$-factorialization by small modifications and rational contractions are bounded. The assertion includes the transforms and images of a bounded marked support, the exceptional loci of the arrows, and their chosen graph resolutions. It also includes a non-extracting birational map followed by a contraction morphism. Here a diagram is a subdiagram of the collection of contraction models and their canonical arrows compatible with the fixed function field.
  - Explanation: Bounded log-Fano sources control compatible contraction diagrams, including actual arrow graphs, marked loci, and resolutions. Compatibility uses fixed function-field identifications rather than arbitrary maps between isomorphic vertices.
- **claim-uniform-geometric-generic-links** (bound, [source](#stmt-bd-diagrams)): Bounded marked families of geometric generic diagrams can be chosen with the following properties:* 1. *There are such families depending only on the dimension so that, for every specified birational map $f:V/B\dashrightarrow V'/B'$ between terminal four-dimensional Mori fibre spaces, there is an ordinary Sarkisov factorization of $f$ whose individual geometric generic link diagrams belong to these families.* 2. *For every fixed rational $0<c<1/4$, there are such families depending only on the dimension and $c$ so that, for every smooth projective threefold $W$ with a reduced SNC divisor $D$ and every two given negative $(K_W+cD)$ programs with Mori fibre-space outputs, there is a connecting log Sarkisov path whose individual geometric generic link diagrams belong to these families.* *In <span class="upright">(i)</span> the path induces the specified map $f$; in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> it induces the birational map determined by the two given maps from $W$. The diagrams retain these identifications of their function fields. For a link with common base $Q$, put $F=\mathbb C(Q)$ and choose an algebraic closure $\overline F$. The bounded diagrams are the restrictions to the geometric generic fibre over $\overline F$. They include the small models, both ends, their base contractions, exceptional loci, marked transforms, and bounded bad loci as in Remark <a href="#bd:bad-loci" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bd:bad-loci">27</a>. In <span class="upright">(i)</span> the marks are the exceptional and bad loci of the link contractions; in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> they also include the restrictions of the transforms of the original divisor $D$. Each induced end-base contraction $B\to Q$ is of Fano type over $Q$. Its geometric generic fibre is integral and has a rationally connected smooth proper model. In <span class="upright">(ii)</span> all marked prime divisors on a link model are transforms of primes on the chosen common start: the maps from that start do not extract. Higher starts with the mark rule of Lemma <a href="#bd:higher-start" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bd:higher-start">24</a> are allowed.* *In both parts the families are independent of the start, its resolution, the birational map, the length of a path, or the K3 test collection.
  - Explanation: Ordinary fourfold maps and fixed-coefficient log threefold programs admit paths with uniformly bounded marked geometric generic link diagrams. The bounds concern individual links and do not accumulate with path length.
- **claim-finite-snc-extraction-list** (construction, [source](#stmt-bd-snc-places)): Let $(R,\Gamma)$ be a smooth pair with SNC support and rational coefficients $b_i<1$, allowing negative coefficients. There are only finitely many exceptional divisorial valuations $F$ with $a(F,R,\Gamma)\leq1$. They are obtained by a finite collection of blow-ups of boundary strata. If the pairs and their SNC components vary in a fixed log-smooth family with a fixed finite coefficient set, all these valuations occur in finitely many such relative constructions after stratification.
  - Explanation: A klt SNC pair has only finitely many exceptional valuations below the stated discrepancy cutoff, obtainable through finitely many stratum blowups. Fixed log-smooth family data make these constructions uniform.
- **claim-bounded-low-discrepancy-terminalizations** (construction, [source](#stmt-bd-terminalization)): Let $(T,\Lambda)$ range over a bounded collection of projective klt log-Fano pairs with fixed finite rational coefficient set and bounded marked support. There is a bounded collection of marked diagrams containing a $\mathbb Q$-factorial crepant terminalization extracting exactly the exceptional valuations $$a(F,T,\Lambda)\leq1.$$ Every normal projective model which extracts only a subset of these valuations, followed by non-extracting birational moves and contraction morphisms, occurs among bounded marked contraction diagrams of these terminalizations. The bound depends on the given family, not on a resolution chosen to construct one of the models.
  - Explanation: Bounded marked log-Fano pairs have bounded crepant terminalization diagrams extracting the entire low-discrepancy list. Models extracting subsets are controlled as non-extracting contractions of those terminalizations.
- **claim-uniform-log-fano-coefficient** (bound, [source](#stmt-bd-coefficient)): Let $(T,D)$ range over a bounded collection with $T$ a projective klt Fano variety and $D$ a reduced divisor. Assume $K_T$ and $D$ are $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and $D$ is numerically proportional to $-K_T$. There is a rational $c_*>0$ such that $(T,cD)$ is klt log Fano for every rational $0<c<c_*$. This also holds when the numerical proportionality is known over a characteristic-zero field before geometric base change and both divisors are defined over that field.
  - Explanation: Bounded reduced boundaries numerically proportional to the anticanonical class admit a uniform positive rational coefficient range preserving klt log-Fano structure, including the stated descent-compatible generic-field case.
- **claim-uniform-choice-order** (construction, [source](#stmt-bd-choice-order)): For the conic-base ends $V/T$ of ordinary fourfold links in the paths of Theorem <a href="#bd:diagrams" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bd:diagrams">28</a>, over $Q$ with $\dim Q\leq1$, a single rational number $$0<c<1/4$$ can be fixed such that the geometric generic pair $(T_{\overline F},c(D_T)_{\overline F})$, with $F=\mathbb C(Q)$, is klt log Fano. Here $D_T$ is the full visible divisorial ramification support defined on the original conic base above. Fix this number before applying the log part of Theorem <a href="#bd:diagrams" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bd:diagrams">28</a> or Theorem <a href="#bd:terminalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bd:terminalization">30</a>. All resulting large-degree exclusions have a common threshold independent of the K3 test subcollection and of the chosen Sarkisov path.
  - Explanation: The ordinary diagrams first determine a common ramification coefficient. Auxiliary log-diagram and extraction bounds are taken only after fixing that coefficient, and then supply a common large-degree exclusion threshold.
- **claim-uniruled-contraction-divisors** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sl-uniruled-vertical)): Let $f:Y\to Z$ be a projective contraction of normal varieties over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Suppose that $Y$ is of Fano type over $Z$. Let $E$ be a prime divisor which is a component of $f^{-1}(f(E))$ and satisfies $\dim E>\dim f(E)$. Then $E$ is uniruled. Consequently:* 1. *if $\dim Y>\dim Z$, every divisor vertical over $Z$ is uniruled;* 2. *if $f$ is birational, every $f$-exceptional prime divisor is uniruled.* *The same conclusions apply to geometric generic fibres of such a contraction and to birational transforms of the divisors.
  - Explanation: Relative Fano type makes the specified positive-dimensional fibre divisors uniruled. This applies to vertical divisors of fibre contractions and exceptional divisors of birational contractions.
- **claim-base-vertical-correction-zero** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sl-base-v-zero)): For every base contraction $B\to Q$ of an ordinary fourfold link, $v(B/Q)=0$.
  - Explanation: Every ordinary link base contraction has vanishing vertical correction: retained primes cancel, and the remaining low-dimensional uniruled divisors have zero test.
- **claim-link-vertical-residual-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sl-vertical)): For every ordinary fourfold link over $Q$ one has $$\begin{aligned} \label{sl:vertical-cocycle} c_{u,\mathrm{vert}/Q}(f)&=v(V'/Q)-v(V/Q),\\ \label{sl:horizontal-residual} c_u(f)-\bigl(v(V'/B')-v(V/B)\bigr) &=c_{u,\mathrm{hor}/Q}(f)-h_Q(V'/B')+h_Q(V/B). \end{aligned}$$ These identities hold for every test degree and require no boundedness threshold.
  - Explanation: Vertical prime valuations give an endpoint difference of vertical corrections. Removing the full node correction leaves the horizontal cocycle adjusted by the horizontal portions of the vertical sums, without any degree threshold.
- **claim-low-and-high-base-residual-vanishing** (bound, [source](#stmt-sl-low-base)): There is an integer $d_{\mathrm{vert}}$, depending only on the bounded marked families in Theorem <a href="#bd:diagrams" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bd:diagrams">28</a> and the exclusions in Theorem <a href="#bd:exclusions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bd:exclusions">22</a>, with the following property. For every $d>d_{\mathrm{vert}}$, every allowed subcollection $\mathcal S$, and every ordinary fourfold link $f$ whose common base satisfies $\dim Q\le1$, one has $$c_{u,\mathrm{hor}/Q}(f)=h_Q(V/B)=h_Q(V'/B')=0.$$ In particular $H_Q(f)=0$. If $\dim Q=3$, these equalities hold for every $d$. The integer $d_{\mathrm{vert}}$ is independent of the link, the birational map, the number of links in a path, and the choice of $\mathcal S$.
  - Explanation: Uniform large-degree exclusions kill horizontal and horizontal-vertical terms over point or curve common bases. Over the larger common-base dimension stated separately, generic curves are isomorphic and no threshold is needed.
- **claim-generic-surface-reduction** (classification, [source](#stmt-sl-generic-surface-reduction)): Suppose $\dim Q=2$. Then $k=\mathbb C(Q)$ is a rational function field in two variables over $\mathbb C$. The generic fourfold-link models are smooth projective geometrically integral surfaces over $k$, and all small modifications in the link become isomorphisms. An end is either a del Pezzo surface of Picard number one over $k$, or a conic bundle over a smooth projective geometrically integral genus-zero curve over $k$, of relative Picard number one. These statements do not assert that the surface or the genus-zero curve is split over $k$. In the conic case the total Picard number of the generic surface over $k$ is two.
  - Explanation: A surface common base is rational. Its generic link becomes a smooth surface problem over the original rational function field, with small modifications becoming isomorphisms and Mori ends retaining their possibly nonsplit structures.
- **claim-generic-structured-dichotomy** (classification, [source](#stmt-sl-generic-dichotomy)): Suppose $\dim Q=2$ and put $k=\mathbb C(Q)$. After identifying the middle generic surfaces by the restricted small maps, let $W$ be their common smooth model. Exactly one of the following reductions applies:* 1. *The restricted link induces an isomorphism of the structured generic ends. In the conic case their curve fields are the same embedded subfield of $k(W)$. Its horizontal cocycle and $H_Q$ vanish.* 2. *The two ends are obtained from $W$ by distinct elementary $K_W$-negative contractions, each either divisorial to a rank-one del Pezzo surface or a conic contraction. Here $\rho(W/k)=2$, the two contractions give the two extremal rays, and $-K_W$ is ample.* *In particular two distinct conic structures on the same surface belong to <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, not <span class="upright">(i)</span>.
  - Explanation: A generic surface link is either a structured isomorphism preserving the embedded conic-base field or a genuine move between the distinct extremal contractions of a del Pezzo middle surface.
- **claim-conic-horizontal-vertical-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sl-conic-h)): For a conic-bundle generic surface $S\to C$, let $\Delta$ be the finite $G_k$-set of singular geometric fibres and let $\widetilde\Delta$ be the double set of their components. Each component cover over a discriminant orbit is nonsplit. The quaternion class of the generic conic over $k(C)$ ramifies exactly at these points, and its residue extensions are exactly these component covers. Moreover, $$\label{sl:h-discriminant} h_Q(V/B)=u(\widetilde\Delta)-u(\Delta)=-u(\Delta).$$ For a rank-one del Pezzo generic surface, $h_Q(V/B)=0$.
  - Explanation: Singular conic fibres have nonsplit quadratic component covers equal to the Brauer residue covers. The component fields have zero test, leaving the negative discriminant-set test as the horizontal vertical correction.
- **claim-del-pezzo-pencil-sets** (classification, [source](#stmt-sl-pencil-sets)): The sets $A(S)$ have respectively five and three elements in degrees five and six. In degree six there is also a two-element set $$A_2(S)=\{C\in\operatorname{Pic}(S_{\overline k}): C^2=1,\ -K_S\cdot C=3\},$$ and an equality of rational $G_k$-representations $$\label{sl:degree-six-representation} \operatorname{Pic}(S_{\overline k})_\mathbb Q\oplus\mathbb Q \simeq\mathbb Q[A(S)]\oplus\mathbb Q[A_2(S)].$$ If $S$ has Picard rank one over $k$, both finite sets in <a href="#sl:degree-six-representation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sl:degree-six-representation">[sl:degree-six-representation]</a> are transitive.
  - Explanation: The stated del Pezzo pencil sets have fixed finite cardinalities. In degree six a second finite set supplies the Picard representation decomposition; rank one over the ground field forces transitivity of both sets.
- **claim-two-ray-surface-endpoint-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sl-surface-identity)): Let $\varphi:V/B\dashrightarrow V'/B'$ be an ordinary fourfold link over a two-dimensional common base $Q$. Put $k=\mathbb C(Q)$, and denote its generic end surfaces, with their induced Mori structures, by $S$ and $S'$. If its restriction is the nontrivial two-ray move in Lemma <a href="#sl:generic-dichotomy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sl:generic-dichotomy">38</a><span class="upright">(ii)</span>, then $$\label{sl:surface-equation} c_{u,\mathrm{hor}/Q}(\varphi)=w(S')-w(S).$$ Every conic surface participating in such a move has at most seven singular geometric fibres. In the other case of Lemma <a href="#sl:generic-dichotomy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sl:generic-dichotomy">38</a>, $H_Q=0$ without any bound on the discriminant size.
  - Explanation: For a genuine generic two-ray move, actual equivariant exceptional and pencil sets identify the horizontal cocycle with the endpoint change of the surface correction. The conic discriminant bound belongs to this nontrivial regime.
- **claim-bounded-ramification-orbit-exclusion** (bound, [source](#stmt-sf-residue-bound)): Suppose that a normal projective model of a genus-zero presentation $L/k$ occurs in a bounded family of diagrams $$Y\longrightarrow S,$$ where $S$ is a normal projective surface model of $k$. Suppose also that the family has bounded marked divisorial support containing the full visible ramification of $\alpha$ on $Y$. The family may instead be given on the geometric generic fibre over a curve $Q$, in which case the diagrams are over $Q$ and $\mathbb C(Q)\subset k$ is required.* *For $r_k\leq7$, the orbit fields in $\Delta_k$, $\widetilde\Delta_k$, and, when defined, $P_k$, are finite covers of degree at most fourteen with branch contained in a uniformly bounded bad locus on the base diagram. Consequently none of these orbit fields is counted by $u$ once $d$ exceeds a constant depending only on the bounded family.
  - Explanation: Bounded diagrams carrying the full visible ramification bound both orbit-cover degrees and branch locations. Hurwitz estimates then exclude all the candidate orbit fields from large-degree K3 tests.
- **claim-nonzero-candidate-embedded-field-uniqueness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sf-unique)): For $d$ above the uniform bound fixed after <a href="#sf:c-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sf:c-choice">[sf:c-choice]</a>, if an eligible $k\subset L$ has $\sigma_k(L,\alpha)\ne0$, then every eligible surface subfield of $(L,\alpha)$ equals $k$ as an embedded subfield of $L$.
  - Explanation: At uniformly large degree a nonzero conic candidate identifies the unique eligible surface subfield as an embedded field, rather than merely determining its abstract isomorphism class.
- **claim-surface-end-correction-vanishing** (bound, [source](#stmt-sf-surface-end)): At an end $V\to B$ with $\dim B=2$ of an ordinary four-dimensional link over a common base $Q$ of dimension at most one, the degree-five or degree-six del Pezzo correction $w$ is zero for uniformly large $d$.
  - Explanation: At surface-base ends over a point or curve common base, bounded finite étale pencil-class covers force the del Pezzo correction to vanish uniformly in large degree.
- **claim-conic-end-candidate-vanishing** (bound, [source](#stmt-sf-conic-end)): Let $V\to T$ be a conic end of an ordinary four-dimensional Sarkisov link with common base $Q$ of dimension at most one. For the uniform large degrees under consideration, no eligible surface subfield of $(\mathbb C(T),\alpha)$ has nonzero candidate.
  - Explanation: At conic ends over a point or curve common base, a bounded low-discrepancy relative minimal model and comparison of log outputs exclude every nonzero eligible-field candidate.
- **claim-intrinsic-node-correction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sf-intrinsic)): For uniformly sufficiently large $d$, the function <a href="#sf:node-function" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sf:node-function">[sf:node-function]</a> is well defined. At a conic node its value equals the candidate of *every* eligible embedded surface field, including fields whose candidate is zero. At either end of an ordinary four-dimensional link with common base of dimension at most one, its value is zero.
  - Explanation: The node correction is well defined at uniformly large degree and equals every eligible presentation's candidate, including zero candidates. It vanishes at ends over the smaller common bases.
- **claim-mori-point-cocycle-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sf-vanishing)): There is a bound $d_0$ with the following property. Let $X_1$ and $X_2$ be normal projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal rationally connected complex fourfolds whose maps to $\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb C$ are Mori fibre spaces. For $d>d_0$, every birational map $f:X_1\dashrightarrow X_2$ satisfies $$c_u(f)=0.$$ The bound is independent of $X_1$, $X_2$, $f$, its Sarkisov factorization, and the chosen degree-$d$ test subcollection.
  - Explanation: Above a uniform degree bound, the cocycle vanishes on every birational map between the stated Mori fourfolds over points. Link contributions telescope as endpoint differences, and both endpoint corrections vanish.
- **claim-integral-lattice-irrationality-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cub-criterion)): There is an integer $d_0$ with the following property. Let $X$ be a smooth complex cubic fourfold, and let $$A_X=H^4(X,\mathbb Z)\cap H^{2,2}(X), \qquad T_X=A_X^\perp\subset H^4(X,\mathbb Z).$$ Suppose that $$\operatorname{rank}T_X=21,\qquad d=\|\det T_X\|>d_0,\qquad \operatorname{End}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(T_X\otimes\mathbb Q)=\mathbb Q.$$ Then $X$ is not rational.
  - Explanation: The rank, integral determinant, and rational Hodge-endomorphism assumptions imply irrationality of a smooth complex cubic. A hypothetical rational parametrization would receive contradictory evaluations of the same divisorial cocycle.
- **claim-very-general-lattice-data** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cub-generic)): For each admissible $d$, a very general point $X\in\mathcal C_d$ satisfies $$\label{cub:generic-data} \operatorname{rank}A_X=2,\quad \operatorname{rank}T_X=21,\quad \|\det T_X\|=d,\quad \operatorname{End}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(T_X\otimes\mathbb Q)=\mathbb Q.$$ Moreover, $T_X\otimes\mathbb Q$ is simple. All these assertions hold simultaneously with <a href="#cub:categorical" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cub:categorical">[cub:categorical]</a> outside a countable union of proper closed algebraic subsets of $\mathcal C_d$.
  - Explanation: In every admissible divisor, the very general cubic has the required algebraic rank, transcendental rank, determinant, scalar Hodge-endomorphism ring, and simplicity, simultaneously with the ordinary categorical K3 equivalence.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-gw-hodge",
        "exact_quote": "The forms on $T_M$ and $T_Z$ are nondegenerate. The rational Hodge structure $T_{M,\\mathbb Q}$ is generated by its $(3,1)$ and $(1,3)$ subspaces, and $T_{Z,\\mathbb Q}$ is generated by its $(2,0)$ and $(0,2)$ subspaces. The only off-diagonal types in the even cohomology of $M$ are $(3,1)$ and $(1,3)$ in degree four. If $i:Z\\hookrightarrow M$ is a surface embedding, then $$i^*T_{M,\\mathbb Q}=0,\\qquad i_*T_{Z,\\mathbb Q}=0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-gw-path",
        "exact_quote": "In either normal-cone degeneration above, consider a genus-zero tree with exactly two variable insertions of Hodge differences $+2$ and $-2$. Orient the path from the first labelled variable marking to the second, and order the two slots at each path vertex in that direction. Suppose that, with this slot order, each vertex tensor belongs to the applicable row of the following table. Then contraction of the path belongs to the row determined by its two remaining ends: $$\\begin{array}{c|c}\n \\text{two end spaces}&\\text{allowed tensors}\\\\ \\hline\n T_{M,\\mathbb Q},T_{M,\\mathbb Q}\n   & (u,v)\\longmapsto q_M(Au,v),\\quad A\\in G_M,\\\\\n W,W&\\mathbb Qq_Z,\\\\\n T_{M,\\mathbb Q},W\\text{ or }W,T_{M,\\mathbb Q}&0.\n \\end{array}$$ One may replace $G_M$ by any specified rational unital operator algebra, provided that each path vertex still satisfies its applicable row in this oriented slot order.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-gw-blocks",
        "exact_quote": "Along a smooth weak factorization starting at $\\mathbb P^4$, each transcendental lattice is an integral orthogonal sum of whole shifted surface-transcendental lattices. Its operator algebra is the product of independent rational scalar algebras on those summands. A blowdown with nonzero surface-transcendental contribution removes one whole summand, whose integral polarized Hodge-isometry class is that of the entire transcendental lattice of its center. A step with zero transcendental contribution leaves the decomposition unchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bd-pencil",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a degree-$d$ K3 surface. If a dominant rational map from $S$ to a smooth projective curve has connected general fibre, that curve is $\\mathbb P^1$. If $g$ is the genus of the smooth general fibre on a resolution of the map, then $$2g-2\\geq\\sqrt d.$$ For an arbitrary dominant rational map to a curve the same inequality holds for every geometric general fibre component, after normalization.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bd-pencil",

          "source_id": "PM-054.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-bd-exclusions",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the finite-type families occurring in each of the following statements, and fix an integer $N\\geq1$. There is an integer $b$, depending only on these families and on $N$, with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *No member of a specified birationally bounded collection of varieties of dimension at most three has a degree-$d$ K3 MRC base with $d>b$.*\n\n2.  *Let $(Y,D)$ range over a bounded collection of projective curve or surface pairs. The smooth projective curves covering the curve members with degree at most $N$, *étale off $D$*, have bounded genus. No surface field obtained by such a cover of a surface member is the field of a degree-$d$ K3 surface for $d>b$.*\n\n3.  *Let $T\\dashrightarrow C$ be a dominant map from an integral complex surface to a smooth projective curve. Suppose each normalized geometric generic fibre component is a degree-at-most-$N$ cover of a member of a fixed bounded collection of curve pairs, *étale off the marked boundary*. Then $T$ is not birational to a degree-$d$ K3 surface for $d>b$.*\n\n4.  *Let $T\\dashrightarrow C$ be a dominant map from an integral complex threefold to a smooth projective curve. Suppose the geometric generic fibre components are uniruled and belong to a fixed birationally bounded collection of surfaces. Then the MRC base of $T$ is not a degree-$d$ K3 surface for $d>b$.*\n\n*The bounded geometric data in <span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span> and <span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> are understood after algebraic closure of $\\mathbb C(C)$; neither the total spaces $T$ nor the maps to $C$ are required to be bounded. The assertions apply to each field factor of a finite algebra separately.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-bd-central",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a wall in this geography on a smooth projective start $(W,\\Phi)$. Assume that $(W,\\Phi)$ is $\\epsilon$-lc. Then the relative small models in the link have a common set of prime valuations and admit a small modification $N^{\\mathrm c}/Q$ such that $$-(K_{N^{\\mathrm c}}+\\Phi_{N^{\\mathrm c}})\n       \\quad\\text{is nef and big over }Q.$$ Its log-anticanonical ample-model map $N^{\\mathrm c}\\to Z^{\\mathrm c}$ is small. Both $N^{\\mathrm c}$ and $Z^{\\mathrm c}$ are $\\epsilon$-lc with their transformed boundaries. All the link vertices and contraction bases are rational contractions of a small $\\mathbb Q$-factorialization of $Z^{\\mathrm c}$. If $\\Phi=0$, all the small models can be taken terminal.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bd-higher-start",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(W,\\Phi)$ be smooth projective with SNC boundary whose coefficients belong to $\\{0,c\\}$, where $0<c<1/4$ and $\\dim W\\leq3$. Suppose two Mori fibre spaces are outputs of negative $(K_W+\\Phi)$ programs. On a higher smooth common start resolving the maps to both endpoints, keep the marks on original prime divisors and assign coefficient either $0$ or $c$ to any new prime divisor, with SNC total support. The same two outputs remain outcomes of the program for this new marked pair. In its geography the original marked discrepancy bound can be taken to be $\\epsilon=1-c$, independently of the higher start. The analogous assertion holds for ordinary terminal Mori fibre spaces with zero boundary and $\\epsilon=1$, including the four-dimensional paths used below.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bd-cartier-strata",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be a bounded collection of marked projective klt log-Fano pairs $(Y,B)$, with coefficients of $B$ in a fixed finite subset of $\\mathbb Q$. Finitely many stratified families, after finite base changes of their parameter spaces, can be chosen so that:*\n\n1.  *the log-canonical divisor is relatively $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier, with a common Cartier multiple on each stratum;*\n\n2.  *there is a simultaneous log resolution of the marked support, and the coefficients of the crepant pullback of $B$ are constant on each stratum;*\n\n3.  *any further specified rational Weil divisor, with bounded marked support and coefficients in a fixed finite subset of $\\mathbb Q$, which is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier for every member has a common Cartier multiple on each stratum, and its exceptional pullback coefficients are constant.*\n\n*The same assertions about $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier classes hold for a bounded collection of varieties with rational singularities whose smooth projective resolutions have $H^1(\\mathcal O)=H^2(\\mathcal O)=0$. In these families $\\mathbb Q$-factoriality is a constructible condition.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bd-cartier-strata",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be a bounded collection of marked projective klt log-Fano pairs $(Y,B)$, with coefficients of $B$ in a fixed finite subset of $\\mathbb Q$. In these families $\\mathbb Q$-factoriality is a constructible condition.",
          "source_id": "PM-054.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bd-contractions",
        "exact_quote": "For a bounded collection of projective klt log-Fano pairs with fixed finite coefficient set, all diagrams obtained from a small $\\mathbb Q$-factorialization by small modifications and rational contractions are bounded. The assertion includes the transforms and images of a bounded marked support, the exceptional loci of the arrows, and their chosen graph resolutions. It also includes a non-extracting birational map followed by a contraction morphism. Here a diagram is a subdiagram of the collection of contraction models and their canonical arrows compatible with the fixed function field.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bd-diagrams",
        "exact_quote": "Bounded marked families of geometric generic diagrams can be chosen with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *There are such families depending only on the dimension so that, for every specified birational map $f:V/B\\dashrightarrow V'/B'$ between terminal four-dimensional Mori fibre spaces, there is an ordinary Sarkisov factorization of $f$ whose individual geometric generic link diagrams belong to these families.*\n\n2.  *For every fixed rational $0<c<1/4$, there are such families depending only on the dimension and $c$ so that, for every smooth projective threefold $W$ with a reduced SNC divisor $D$ and every two given negative $(K_W+cD)$ programs with Mori fibre-space outputs, there is a connecting log Sarkisov path whose individual geometric generic link diagrams belong to these families.*\n\n*In <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> the path induces the specified map $f$; in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> it induces the birational map determined by the two given maps from $W$. The diagrams retain these identifications of their function fields. For a link with common base $Q$, put $F=\\mathbb C(Q)$ and choose an algebraic closure $\\overline F$. The bounded diagrams are the restrictions to the geometric generic fibre over $\\overline F$. They include the small models, both ends, their base contractions, exceptional loci, marked transforms, and bounded bad loci as in Remark <a href=\"#bd:bad-loci\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bd:bad-loci\">27</a>. In <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> the marks are the exceptional and bad loci of the link contractions; in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> they also include the restrictions of the transforms of the original divisor $D$. Each induced end-base contraction $B\\to Q$ is of Fano type over $Q$. Its geometric generic fibre is integral and has a rationally connected smooth proper model. In <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> all marked prime divisors on a link model are transforms of primes on the chosen common start: the maps from that start do not extract. Higher starts with the mark rule of Lemma <a href=\"#bd:higher-start\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bd:higher-start\">24</a> are allowed.*\n\n*In both parts the families are independent of the start, its resolution, the birational map, the length of a path, or the K3 test collection.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bd-diagrams",
          "exact_quote": "Bounded marked families of geometric generic diagrams can be chosen with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *There are such families depending only on the dimension so that, for every specified birational map $f:V/B\\dashrightarrow V'/B'$ between terminal four-dimensional Mori fibre spaces, there is an ordinary Sarkisov factorization of $f$ whose individual geometric generic link diagrams belong to these families.*\n\n2.  *For every fixed rational $0<c<1/4$, there are such families depending only on the dimension and $c$ so that, for every smooth projective threefold $W$ with a reduced SNC divisor $D$ and every two given negative $(K_W+cD)$ programs with Mori fibre-space outputs, there is a connecting log Sarkisov path whose individual geometric generic link diagrams belong to these families.*\n\n*In <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> the path induces the specified map $f$; in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> it induces the birational map determined by the two given maps from $W$. The diagrams retain these identifications of their function fields. For a link with common base $Q$, put $F=\\mathbb C(Q)$ and choose an algebraic closure $\\overline F$. The bounded diagrams are the restrictions to the geometric generic fibre over $\\overline F$. They include the small models, both ends, their base contractions, exceptional loci, marked transforms, and bounded bad loci as in Remark <a href=\"#bd:bad-loci\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bd:bad-loci\">27</a>. In <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> the marks are the exceptional and bad loci of the link contractions; in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> they also include the restrictions of the transforms of the original divisor $D$. Each induced end-base contraction $B\\to Q$ is of Fano type over $Q$. Its geometric generic fibre is integral and has a rationally connected smooth proper model. In <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> all marked prime divisors on a link model are transforms of primes on the chosen common start: the maps from that start do not extract. Higher starts with the mark rule of Lemma <a href=\"#bd:higher-start\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bd:higher-start\">24</a> are allowed.*\n\n*In both parts the families are independent of the start, its resolution, the birational map, the length of a path, or the K3 test collection.",
          "source_id": "PM-054.1"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-bd-snc-places",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(R,\\Gamma)$ be a smooth pair with SNC support and rational coefficients $b_i<1$, allowing negative coefficients. There are only finitely many exceptional divisorial valuations $F$ with $a(F,R,\\Gamma)\\leq1$. They are obtained by a finite collection of blow-ups of boundary strata.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-bd-terminalization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,\\Lambda)$ range over a bounded collection of projective klt log-Fano pairs with fixed finite rational coefficient set and bounded marked support. There is a bounded collection of marked diagrams containing a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial crepant terminalization extracting exactly the exceptional valuations $$a(F,T,\\Lambda)\\leq1.$$ Every normal projective model which extracts only a subset of these valuations, followed by non-extracting birational moves and contraction morphisms, occurs among bounded marked contraction diagrams of these terminalizations.         "exact_quote": "Let $(T,D)$ range over a bounded collection with $T$ a projective klt Fano variety and $D$ a reduced divisor.         "anchor": "stmt-bd-choice-order",
        "exact_quote": "For the conic-base ends $V/T$ of ordinary fourfold links in the paths of Theorem <a href=\"#bd:diagrams\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bd:diagrams\">28</a>, over $Q$ with $\\dim Q\\leq1$, a single rational number $$0<c<1/4$$ can be fixed such that the geometric generic pair $(T_{\\overline F},c(D_T)_{\\overline F})$, with $F=\\mathbb C(Q)$, is klt log Fano.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sl-uniruled-vertical",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:Y\\to Z$ be a projective contraction of normal varieties over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Suppose that $Y$ is of Fano type over $Z$. Let $E$ be a prime divisor which is a component of $f^{-1}(f(E))$ and satisfies $\\dim E>\\dim f(E)$. Then $E$ is uniruled. Consequently:*\n\n1.  *if $\\dim Y>\\dim Z$, every divisor vertical over $Z$ is uniruled;*\n\n2.  *if $f$ is birational, every $f$-exceptional prime divisor is uniruled.*\n\n*The same conclusions apply to geometric generic fibres of such a contraction and to birational transforms of the divisors.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sl-uniruled-vertical",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:Y\\to Z$ be a projective contraction of normal varieties over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Suppose that $Y$ is of Fano type over $Z$. Consequently:*\n\n1.  *if $\\dim Y>\\dim Z$, every divisor vertical over $Z$ is uniruled;*\n\n2.  *if $f$ is birational, every $f$-exceptional prime divisor is uniruled.*\n\n*The same conclusions apply to geometric generic fibres of such a contraction and to birational transforms of the divisors.",
          "source_id": "PM-054.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $f:Y\\to Z$ be a projective contraction of normal varieties over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. Suppose that $Y$ is of Fano type over $Z$.         "exact_quote": "For every ordinary fourfold link over $Q$ one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{sl:vertical-cocycle}\n c_{u,\\mathrm{vert}/Q}(f)&=v(V'/Q)-v(V/Q),\\\\\n\\label{sl:horizontal-residual}\n c_u(f)-\\bigl(v(V'/B')-v(V/B)\\bigr)\n &=c_{u,\\mathrm{hor}/Q}(f)-h_Q(V'/B')+h_Q(V/B).\n\\end{aligned}$$ These identities hold for every test degree and require no boundedness threshold.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-054.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every ordinary fourfold link over $Q$ one has $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{sl:vertical-cocycle}\n c_{u,\\mathrm{vert}/Q}(f)&=v(V'/Q)-v(V/Q),\\\\\n\\label{sl:horizontal-residual}\n c_u(f)-\\bigl(v(V'/B')-v(V/B)\\bigr)\n &=c_{u,\\mathrm{hor}/Q}(f)-h_Q(V'/B')+h_Q(V/B).\n\\end{aligned}$$ These identities hold for every test degree and require no boundedness threshold.",
      "explanation": "Vertical prime valuations give an endpoint difference of vertical corrections. Removing the full node correction leaves the horizontal cocycle adjusted by the horizontal portions of the vertical sums, without any degree threshold.",

        "exact_quote": "There is an integer $d_{\\mathrm{vert}}$, depending only on the bounded marked families in Theorem <a href=\"#bd:diagrams\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bd:diagrams\">28</a> and the exclusions in Theorem <a href=\"#bd:exclusions\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bd:exclusions\">22</a>, with the following property. For every $d>d_{\\mathrm{vert}}$, every allowed subcollection $\\mathcal S$, and every ordinary fourfold link $f$ whose common base satisfies $\\dim Q\\le1$, one has $$c_{u,\\mathrm{hor}/Q}(f)=h_Q(V/B)=h_Q(V'/B')=0.$$ In particular $H_Q(f)=0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sl-generic-surface-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\dim Q=2$. Then $k=\\mathbb C(Q)$ is a rational function field in two variables over $\\mathbb C$. The generic fourfold-link models are smooth projective geometrically integral surfaces over $k$, and all small modifications in the link become isomorphisms. An end is either a del Pezzo surface of Picard number one over $k$, or a conic bundle over a smooth projective geometrically integral genus-zero curve over $k$, of relative Picard number one. These statements do not assert that the surface or the genus-zero curve is split over $k$. In the conic case the total Picard number of the generic surface over $k$ is two.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sl-generic-dichotomy",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $\\dim Q=2$ and put $k=\\mathbb C(Q)$. After identifying the middle generic surfaces by the restricted small maps, let $W$ be their common smooth model. Exactly one of the following reductions applies:*\n\n1.  *The restricted link induces an isomorphism of the structured generic ends. In the conic case their curve fields are the same embedded subfield of $k(W)$. Its horizontal cocycle and $H_Q$ vanish.*\n\n2.  *The two ends are obtained from $W$ by distinct elementary $K_W$-negative contractions, each either divisorial to a rank-one del Pezzo surface or a conic contraction. Here $\\rho(W/k)=2$, the two contractions give the two extremal rays, and $-K_W$ is ample.*\n\n*In particular two distinct conic structures on the same surface belong to <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, not <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span>.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sl-conic-h",
        "exact_quote": "For a conic-bundle generic surface $S\\to C$, let $\\Delta$ be the finite $G_k$-set of singular geometric fibres and let $\\widetilde\\Delta$ be the double set of their components. Each component cover over a discriminant orbit is nonsplit. The quaternion class of the generic conic over $k(C)$ ramifies exactly at these points, and its residue extensions are exactly these component covers. Moreover, $$\\label{sl:h-discriminant}\n h_Q(V/B)=u(\\widetilde\\Delta)-u(\\Delta)=-u(\\Delta).$$ For a rank-one del Pezzo generic surface, $h_Q(V/B)=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sl-conic-h",
          "exact_quote": "For a conic-bundle generic surface $S\\to C$, let $\\Delta$ be the finite $G_k$-set of singular geometric fibres and let $\\widetilde\\Delta$ be the double set of their components. Moreover, $$\\label{sl:h-discriminant}\n h_Q(V/B)=u(\\widetilde\\Delta)-u(\\Delta)=-u(\\Delta).$$ For a rank-one del Pezzo generic surface, $h_Q(V/B)=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-054.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-sl-pencil-sets",
        "exact_quote": "The sets $A(S)$ have respectively five and three elements in degrees five and six.         "source_id": "PM-054.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sl-surface-identity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\varphi:V/B\\dashrightarrow V'/B'$ be an ordinary fourfold link over a two-dimensional common base $Q$. Put $k=\\mathbb C(Q)$, and denote its generic end surfaces, with their induced Mori structures, by $S$ and $S'$. If its restriction is the nontrivial two-ray move in Lemma <a href=\"#sl:generic-dichotomy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sl:generic-dichotomy\">38</a><span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, then $$\\label{sl:surface-equation}\n c_{u,\\mathrm{hor}/Q}(\\varphi)=w(S')-w(S).$$ Every conic surface participating in such a move has at most seven singular geometric fibres. In the other case of Lemma <a href=\"#sl:generic-dichotomy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sl:generic-dichotomy\">38</a>, $H_Q=0$ without any bound on the discriminant size.",
        "source_id": "PM-054.1"
      },

In the other case of Lemma <a href=\"#sl:generic-dichotomy\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sl:generic-dichotomy\">38</a>, $H_Q=0$ without any bound on the discriminant size.",

        "anchor": "stmt-sf-residue-bound",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that a normal projective model of a genus-zero presentation $L/k$ occurs in a bounded family of diagrams $$Y\\longrightarrow S,$$ where $S$ is a normal projective surface model of $k$. Suppose also that the family has bounded marked divisorial support containing the full visible ramification of $\\alpha$ on $Y$. The family may instead be given on the geometric generic fibre over a curve $Q$, in which case the diagrams are over $Q$ and $\\mathbb C(Q)\\subset k$ is required.*\n\n*For $r_k\\leq7$, the orbit fields in $\\Delta_k$, $\\widetilde\\Delta_k$, and, when defined, $P_k$, are finite covers of degree at most fourteen with branch contained in a uniformly bounded bad locus on the base diagram. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Discriminant labelling and Hassett locus** ([source](#cub:admissible)): A *labelling of discriminant $d$* is a saturated positive-definite rank-two sublattice $$K\subset H^4(X,\mathbb Z)\cap H^{2,2}(X)$$ containing $h^2$, with $\det K=d$.
- **Admissible discriminant** ([source](#cub:admissible)): We call an integer $d$ *admissible* if $$\label{cub:admissible} d>6,\qquad d\equiv0,2\pmod6,\qquad 4\nmid d,\quad 9\nmid d,\quad p\nmid d\ \text{for every odd prime }p\equiv2\pmod3.$$
- **Associated polarized K3 surface** ([source](#cub:admissible)): Here an associated K3 surface has a primitive polarization $L$ of degree $d$ and an integral Hodge isometry $K^\perp\simeq L^\perp(-1)$, where the surface cup pairing is reversed.
- **Maximal rationally connected quotient** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a smooth projective variety $V$, write $V\dashrightarrow R(V)$ for its maximal rationally connected quotient. Its general fibres are rationally connected, its base is not uniruled, and the base is determined up to birational equivalence.
- **K3 test collection** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Fix a positive integer $d>2$ and any collection $\mathcal S$ of isomorphism classes of smooth projective K3 surfaces $S$ such that $$\label{pre:test-collection} \operatorname{Pic}(S)=\mathbb ZL_S,\qquad L_S\text{ is ample},\qquad L_S^2=d,\qquad \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathbb C}(S)=\{1\}.$$
- **MRC K3 test** ([source](#stmt-pre-test)): For a finitely generated field $F/\mathbb C$, choose a smooth projective model $V$ and set $$u(F)= \begin{cases} 1,&\text{if }R(V)\text{ is birational to some }S\in\mathcal S,\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$ For an integral variety $V$, put $u(V)=u(\mathbb C(V))$.
- **Finite étale orbit test** ([source](#pre:finite-orbits)): If $k/\mathbb C$ is finitely generated and a finite étale $k$-algebra is $A=\prod_i k_i$, define $$\label{pre:finite-orbits} u(A)=\sum_i u(k_i).$$
- **Divisorial cocycle** ([source](#pre:cocycle-definition)): For a birational map $f:X\dashrightarrow Y$, identify the function fields by $f^*: \mathbb C(Y)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C(X)=F$ and define $$\label{pre:cocycle-definition} c_u(f)= \sum_{\nu\in\mathcal D_F(Y)\setminus\mathcal D_F(X)}u(\kappa(\nu)) -\sum_{\nu\in\mathcal D_F(X)\setminus\mathcal D_F(Y)}u(\kappa(\nu)).$$
- **Integral transcendental lattices and pairing convention** ([source](#pm-h-12)): If $M$ is a rationally connected smooth projective fourfold, set $$L_M=H^4(M,\mathbb Z)/\mathrm{tors},\qquad T_M=L_M\cap H^4(M,\mathbb Q)_{\mathrm{Hdg}}^{\perp}.$$
- **Genus-zero transcendental operator algebra** ([source](#stmt-gw-algebra)): For every invariant $$B(u,v)= \left\langle\tau_a(u),\tau_b(v), \tau_{a_1}(\gamma_1),\ldots,\tau_{a_n}(\gamma_n) \right\rangle^M_{0,\beta},\qquad u,v\in T_{M,\mathbb Q},$$ let $A_B\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb Q}(T_{M,\mathbb Q})$ be determined by $B(u,v)=q_M(A_Bu,v)$. Let $G_M$ be the unital rational algebra generated by all such operators. On the zero space we take the zero algebra.
- **Vertical divisor correction** ([source](#sl:v-definition)): We define $$\label{sl:v-definition} v(Y/Z)= \sum_{E\in\operatorname{Div}_{Z}(Y)}u(E) -\sum_{D\in\operatorname{Div}(Z)}u(D).$$
- **Horizontal portion of the vertical correction** ([source](#sl:h-definition)): For a tower $V\to B\to Q$, let $h_Q(V/B)$ be the portion of <a href="#sl:v-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sl:v-definition">[sl:v-definition]</a> consisting of divisors dominating $Q$: $$\label{sl:h-definition} h_Q(V/B)= \sum_{\substack{E\in\operatorname{Div}_{B}(V)\\E\to Q\ { dominant}}}u(E) -\sum_{\substack{D\in\operatorname{Div}(B)\\D\to Q\ { dominant}}}u(D).$$
- **Structured generic surface correction** ([source](#stmt-sl-w-def)): For a generic Mori surface with its specified structure, set $$w(S)= \begin{cases} u(A(S)),&S\text{ is a rank-one del Pezzo of degree }5\text{ or }6,\\ \tfrac12u(P_\Delta),&S\to C\text{ is a conic bundle and }\|\Delta\|=3,\\ 0,&\text{otherwise}. \end{cases}$$ The del Pezzo value depends on the surface; the conic value includes its chosen genus-zero curve structure.
- **Eligible embedded surface field** ([source](#pm-h-35)): A subfield $k\subset L$ is *eligible* if it is a surface function field and $L$ is the function field of a smooth projective geometrically integral genus-zero curve $C_k$ over $k$, with the following ramification bound. Let $\Delta_k\subset C_k$ be the reduced finite subscheme of closed points at which $\alpha$ has nonzero residue.
- **Conic presentation candidate** ([source](#sf:candidate)): Set $$\label{sf:candidate} \sigma_k(L,\alpha) =u(\Delta_k)+\mathbf{1}_{r_k=3}\,\frac12u(P_k).$$
- **Intrinsic Mori-node correction** ([source](#sf:node-function)): Define a function on the entire node by $$\label{sf:node-function} s(V/B)= \begin{cases} 0,&\dim B\leq1,\\ w(V/B),&\dim B=2,\\ \sigma_k(\mathbb C(B),\alpha),&\dim B=3\text{ and some eligible }k \text{ has nonzero candidate},\\ 0,&\dim B=3\text{ and no such }k\text{ exists}. \end{cases}$$

<!-- PM:END pm.objects.v2 -->

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-irrationality-threshold | Admissible discriminant $d$ | > | $d_0$ | The same threshold applies across the admissible discriminants. |
| q-transcendental-rank | rank of T_X | = | $21$ | The exceptional sets are taken separately in each divisor. |
| q-integral-transcendental-determinant | absolute value of the determinant of T_X | = | $d$ | The determinant equals the labelling discriminant. |
| q-smooth-factorization-test | $c_u(f)$ | = | $1$ | Conditional on the hypothetical birational map and all prescribed lattice hypotheses. |
| q-mori-factorization-test | $c_u(f)$ | = | $0$ | Uniform in the map, factorization, endpoints, and degree-$d$ test subcollection. |
| q-k3-pencil-genus | 2g - 2 | ≥ | $\sqrt d$ | The genus is measured on a resolution, componentwise after normalization when necessary. |
| q-bounded-mrc-degree | K3 polarization degree $d$ | ≤ | $b$ | The bound depends on the collection and does not bound individual quotient maps. |
| q-marked-cover-hurwitz | 2g(A') - 2 | ≤ | $e(2g_A-2)+(e-1)r$ | The cover degree and number of permitted branch points are bounded. |
| q-wall-relative-picard-rank | rho(N_i/Q) | = | $2$ | Ranks are taken before extending the common base function field. |
| q-log-boundary-coefficient | Rational boundary coefficient $c$ | < | $\min\{1/4,c_*\}$ | It is fixed before taking auxiliary log-diagram bounds and the final degree threshold. |
| q-log-generic-curve-degree | degree of K + Phi | = | $-2+cr_k$ | The ramification degree is measured before extension of constants. |
| q-eligible-ramification-degree | $r_k$ | ≤ | $7$ | Counts geometric points of the original ramification support, not the support recomputed after extending constants. |
| q-conic-discriminant-size | absolute value of Delta | = | $8-D$ | The formula counts singular geometric fibres. |
| q-residue-orbit-cover-degree | Degree of a residue or component-choice orbit cover | ≤ | $14$ | The branch locus is contained in a uniformly bounded marked bad locus. |
| q-low-discrepancy-extraction-cutoff | a(F, T, Lambda) | ≤ | $1$ | The entire exceptional extraction list is included, also when an extracted prime has zero crepant boundary coefficient. |
| q-hilbert-square-discriminant-family | Hilbert-square subfamily discriminant $d$ | = | $2n^2+2n+2$ | The sequence is infinite; irrationality requires exceeding the ineffective threshold. |
