<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-mrc-test-invariance** [claim](#stmt-pre-mrc-fibration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-mrc-fibration): Compose with the MRC quotient and use the rationally connected fibration theorem and uniqueness of the quotient. -> MRC invariance of the test and its projective-bundle and dimension consequences.
- **step-small-orbit-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-pre-small-orbits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-small-orbits): Convert a field automorphism into a birational K3 automorphism and use triviality of the test surfaces' automorphism groups. -> Vanishing of the small-orbit and automorphism-bearing field tests.
- **step-divisorial-additivity** [claim](#stmt-pre-cocycle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pre-cocycle): Identify finite symmetric differences of valuation sets and telescope their indicator functions; evaluate exceptional projective bundles over their centers. -> A finite additive cocycle with the extraction-positive blowup sign.
- **step-smooth-factorization** [claim](#stmt-pre-weak-factorization) / [proof](#pm-h-10): Use the cited projective weak factorization theorem and omit smooth divisor blowups. -> A finite zigzag with smooth connected centers of the required codimension.
- **step-hodge-generation** [claim](#stmt-gw-hodge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gw-hodge): Combine rational connectedness, Lefschetz polarizations, and semisimplicity to generate the transcendental structures from off-diagonal types. -> Nondegenerate pairings, Hodge generation, and vanishing restriction and Gysin maps.
- **step-local-tensor-rule** [claim](#stmt-gw-local) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gw-local): For positive projected degree use non-uniruledness and vanishing of holomorphic forms on evaluation images; for zero projected degree apply the projection formula. -> Scalar local cup tensors and positive-projection vanishing.
- **step-oriented-path-contraction** [claim](#stmt-gw-path) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gw-path): Prune diagonal side branches and contract the ordered path using inverse cup forms, composing operators in path order. -> Closure of the allowed tensor rows under path contraction.
- **step-relative-center-reconstruction** [claim](#stmt-gw-relative) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gw-relative): Translate relative conditions into absolute descendants and use simultaneous strong induction with a nonzero fibre coefficient. -> The restricted relative tensor rows.
- **step-divisor-reconstruction** [claim](#stmt-gw-divisor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gw-divisor): Use divisor degeneration and a bounded decreasing relative-list order at fixed contact, with positive leading fibre coefficients. -> Relative old operators, scalar exceptional tensors, and zero mixed tensors.
- **step-integral-operator-splitting** [claim](#stmt-gw-blowup) / [proof](#gw:section): Combine the classical integral blowup formula with relative reconstruction, separate the exceptional factor by its line projector, and recover the old algebra. -> The integral transcendental splitting and independent algebra factors.
- **step-whole-block-cancellation** [claim](#stmt-gw-blocks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gw-blocks): Track primitive central idempotents of the scalar product algebra and recover each integral lattice by intersecting its rational subspace with the ambient integral lattice. -> Blowdowns remove entire saturated integral surface blocks.
- **step-classify-surface-centers** [claim](#stmt-gw-k3-center) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gw-k3-center): Apply Noether's formula and surface classification, then unimodular discriminant gluing and faithful K3 cohomological action. -> A Picard-number-one K3 minimal model of the exact discriminant degree, with trivial automorphisms under the further hypotheses.
- **step-net-smooth-contribution** [claim](#stmt-gw-net-center) / [proof](#proof-stmt-gw-net-center): Use the final off-diagonal Hodge dimension to leave a single whole block and count additions minus removals in its integral Hodge-isometry class. -> The nonzero smooth-factorization cocycle evaluation forced by rationality.
- **step-pencil-genus-lower-bound** [claim](#stmt-bd-pencil) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-pencil): Resolve the pencil by point blowups and combine adjunction, the zero fibre square, and the multiplicity-square inequality. -> The polarization-dependent lower genus bound.
- **step-bound-mrc-k3-degrees** [claim](#stmt-bd-bounded-mrc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-bounded-mrc): Bound minimal surface degrees by intersections; for threefolds use noetherian stabilization of free-curve loci and bounded Hilbert families of MRC fibres. -> A uniform bound on K3 MRC degrees.
- **step-bounded-data-exclusions** [claim](#stmt-bd-exclusions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-exclusions): Apply marked-cover Hurwitz bounds, hyperplane pencils, and bounded irregularities, comparing them with the K3 pencil lower bound. -> Uniform exclusions of large-degree K3 test fields in all stated regimes.
- **step-central-model-construction** [claim](#stmt-bd-central) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-central): Subtract the ample geography difference in discrepancy comparisons and pass through the interior movable class to a small anticanonical ample model. -> A central small model retaining the link valuations and discrepancy bound.
- **step-higher-start-stability** [claim](#stmt-bd-higher-start) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-higher-start): Use the SNC discrepancy estimate and negativity to recover both endpoints, then perturb boundaries into a common geography. -> Endpoint preservation with a resolution-independent discrepancy bound.
- **step-cartier-stratification** [claim](#stmt-bd-cartier-strata) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-cartier-strata): Fix relative numerical divisor data after base change, apply the numerical Cartier criterion, spread the resulting equalities, and use noetherian induction. -> Finite strata with common Cartier multiples and constant pullback coefficients.
- **step-bound-contraction-diagrams** [claim](#stmt-bd-contractions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-contractions): Spread the finite generic Mori chamber and canonical contraction list, use family compatibility to retain completeness, and include graphs and marked resolutions. -> Bounded full marked contraction diagrams.
- **step-bound-geometric-generic-links** [claim](#stmt-bd-diagrams) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-diagrams): Apply boundedness to central geometric generic log-Fano models, bound the marked boundary degree, and recover the link contractions and bad loci. -> Uniform marked ordinary and log geometric generic link families.
- **step-finite-snc-places** [claim](#stmt-bd-snc-places) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-snc-places): Use positive SNC discrepancy weights to bound valuation orders and realize the remaining monomial valuations by stratum blowups. -> A finite low-discrepancy extraction list, uniform in fixed log-smooth families.
- **step-bound-terminalization-diagrams** [claim](#stmt-bd-terminalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-terminalization): Extract the entire finite exceptional list crepantly, spread the terminalizations, and treat subset extractions as non-extracting birational contractions. -> Bounded terminalization and subsequent contraction diagrams independent of arbitrary resolutions.
- **step-bound-log-coefficient** [claim](#stmt-bd-coefficient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-coefficient): Bound klt thresholds on simultaneous resolutions and use numerical proportionality with bounded Cartier indices to preserve anticanonical ampleness. -> A common positive rational coefficient range.
- **step-fix-uniform-choice-order** [claim](#stmt-bd-choice-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bd-choice-order): Use the ordinary rank tower to obtain original-field rank one and numerical proportionality, fix the coefficient, and only then take auxiliary log bounds. -> A noncircular order for the coefficient, auxiliary families, and large-degree threshold.
- **step-uniruled-vertical-divisors** [claim](#stmt-sl-uniruled-vertical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sl-uniruled-vertical): Apply rational chain connectedness of relative log-Fano fibres and select rational curves through general points of the specified fibre components. -> Uniruledness of the required vertical and exceptional divisors.
- **step-base-vertical-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-sl-base-v-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sl-base-v-zero): Cancel retained prime valuations and use uniruledness and dimension to kill the remaining tests. -> Vanishing of the base vertical correction.
- **step-vertical-residual-identities** [claim](#stmt-sl-vertical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sl-vertical): Cancel common vertical valuations and substitute the correction identity for a contraction tower. -> The vertical endpoint difference and horizontal residual formula at every test degree.
- **step-other-base-dimensions** [claim](#stmt-sl-low-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sl-low-base): Use bounded marked components and large-degree K3 exclusions over points and curves; use generic-curve isomorphisms in the remaining stated dimension. -> Vanishing horizontal residuals outside the surface common-base case.
- **step-reduce-to-generic-surfaces** [claim](#stmt-sl-generic-surface-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sl-generic-surface-reduction): Use rational connectedness to make the common surface base rational, restrict to the smooth generic surfaces, and compute Picard ranks over the original field. -> Possibly nonsplit del Pezzo or conic-bundle generic ends with small maps becoming isomorphisms.
- **step-generic-link-dichotomy** [claim](#stmt-sl-generic-dichotomy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sl-generic-dichotomy): Compare the end contractions in the invariant numerical plane and use uniqueness for equal extremal rays. -> A structured isomorphism or a genuine del Pezzo two-ray move.
- **step-conic-horizontal-correction** [claim](#stmt-sl-conic-h) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sl-conic-h): Classify singular conic fibres, prove nonsplitting from relative rank, compute quaternion residues locally, and cancel smooth fibres. -> The horizontal vertical correction expressed by the ramification discriminant set.
- **step-del-pezzo-pencil-sets** [claim](#stmt-sl-pencil-sets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sl-pencil-sets): Enumerate the defining classes in a plane blowup basis and use the Picard representation and invariant rank to prove transitivity. -> Finite pencil-set cardinalities and the degree-six representation decomposition.
- **step-two-ray-surface-identity** [claim](#stmt-sl-surface-identity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sl-surface-identity): Exhaust the intersection-theoretic possibilities and construct actual equivariant bijections of the exceptional and pencil sets, retaining their orbit fields. -> The horizontal cocycle endpoint identity and the nontrivial conic discriminant bound.
- **step-bounded-residue-exclusion** [claim](#stmt-sf-residue-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sf-residue-bound): Use the secondary residue complex and purity to find an unramified open, bound the finite orbit covers, and compare Hurwitz genera with K3 pencils. -> Uniform large-degree vanishing of all candidate orbit-field tests.
- **step-eligible-field-uniqueness** [claim](#stmt-sf-unique) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sf-unique): Connect the eligible presentations by a log path and apply the bounded-residue exclusion at the first departure from the original embedded surface field. -> A nonzero candidate forces uniqueness of the eligible embedded field.
- **step-surface-end-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-sf-surface-end) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sf-surface-end): Realize the finite pencil-class set in the relative Picard scheme over the smooth locus and apply marked-cover exclusions to each field factor. -> Uniform vanishing of the surface-end correction.
- **step-conic-end-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-sf-conic-end) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sf-conic-end): Run a relative log minimal program over the conic base, use negativity to retain only low-discrepancy extractions, and compare the bounded output with the eligible-field output. -> Vanishing of every eligible candidate at a conic end over a point or curve common base.
- **step-intrinsic-node-function** [claim](#stmt-sf-intrinsic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sf-intrinsic): Separate existence of a nonzero candidate from universal candidate vanishing and apply embedded-field uniqueness in the nonzero case. -> An intrinsic correction agreeing with every eligible candidate and vanishing at the required ends.
- **step-mori-cocycle-telescoping** [claim](#stmt-sf-vanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sf-vanishing): Check each common-base dimension, identify each link with an endpoint difference of the combined node corrections, and sum using cocycle additivity. -> Uniform cocycle vanishing between Mori fourfolds over points.
- **step-cubic-irrationality-criterion** [claim](#stmt-cub-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cub-criterion): Assume a rational parametrization, select the same full integral Hodge-isometry test collection in both factorizations, and contradict their cocycle evaluations. -> The uniform lattice-based irrationality criterion.
- **step-very-general-lattice** [claim](#stmt-cub-generic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cub-generic): Exclude rational Hodge-class and nonscalar endomorphism loci on a finite marked cover, use unimodularity for the determinant, and descend the exceptional sets. -> Simultaneous very-general lattice data and categorical equivalence in each admissible divisor.
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cub-generic-2): Apply the uniform irrationality criterion to the simultaneous very-general lattice and categorical locus. -> Irrationality with an ordinary K3 derived equivalence on every sufficiently large admissible divisor.
- **step-associated-k3-consequence** [claim](#stmt-cor-associated-k3) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cub-generic-3): Combine the associated polarized K3 Hodge isometry with irrationality and verify that its inclusion into primitive cubic cohomology is primitive. -> Failure of associated-K3 sufficiency for rationality.
- **step-hilbert-square-consequence** [claim](#stmt-cor-hilbert-square) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cub-generic-4): Use the stated discriminant family in the Hilbert-square criterion, verify admissibility through that criterion, and combine its unboundedness with the main theorem. -> Infinitely many irrational very-general cubics with K3 Hilbert-square Fano varieties.
- **step-integral-chow-consequence** [claim](#stmt-cor-chow-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cub-generic-5): Identify the labelling discriminant with the discriminant in the integral zero-cycle criterion and use admissibility together with the main theorem. -> Irrational cubics with universally trivial zero-cycles and integral Chow decomposition of the diagonal.

On a higher smooth common start resolving the maps to both endpoints, keep the marks on original prime divisors and assign coefficient either $0$ or $c$ to any new prime divisor, with SNC total support. The same two outputs remain outcomes of the program for this new marked pair. In its geography the original marked discrepancy bound can be taken to be $\\epsilon=1-c$, independently of the higher start. The analogous assertion holds for ordinary terminal Mori fibre spaces with zero boundary and $\\epsilon=1$, including the four-dimensional paths used below.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-054.1"
        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bd-cartier-strata",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal P$ be a bounded collection of marked projective klt log-Fano pairs $(Y,B)$, with coefficients of $B$ in a fixed finite subset of $\\mathbb Q$. Finitely many stratified families, after finite base changes of their parameter spaces, can be chosen so that:*\n\n1.  *the log-canonical divisor is relatively $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier, with a common Cartier multiple on each stratum;*\n\n2.  *there is a simultaneous log resolution of the marked support, and the coefficients of the crepant pullback of $B$ are constant on each stratum;*\n\n3.  *any further specified rational Weil divisor, with bounded marked support and coefficients in a fixed finite subset of $\\mathbb Q$, which is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier for every member has a common Cartier multiple on each stratum, and its exceptional pullback coefficients are constant.*\n\n*The same assertions about $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier classes hold for a bounded collection of varieties with rational singularities whose smooth projective resolutions have $H^1(\\mathcal O)=H^2(\\mathcal O)=0$. In these families $\\mathbb Q$-factoriality is a constructible condition.",

        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bd-diagrams",
          "exact_quote": "Bounded marked families of geometric generic diagrams can be chosen with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *There are such families depending only on the dimension so that, for every specified birational map $f:V/B\\dashrightarrow V'/B'$ between terminal four-dimensional Mori fibre spaces, there is an ordinary Sarkisov factorization of $f$ whose individual geometric generic link diagrams belong to these families.*\n\n2.  *For every fixed rational $0<c<1/4$, there are such families depending only on the dimension and $c$ so that, for every smooth projective threefold $W$ with a reduced SNC divisor $D$ and every two given negative $(K_W+cD)$ programs with Mori fibre-space outputs, there is a connecting log Sarkisov path whose individual geometric generic link diagrams belong to these families.*\n\n*In <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> the path induces the specified map $f$; in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> it induces the birational map determined by the two given maps from $W$. The diagrams retain these identifications of their function fields. For a link with common base $Q$, put $F=\\mathbb C(Q)$ and choose an algebraic closure $\\overline F$. The bounded diagrams are the restrictions to the geometric generic fibre over $\\overline F$. They include the small models, both ends, their base contractions, exceptional loci, marked transforms, and bounded bad loci as in Remark <a href=\"#bd:bad-loci\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bd:bad-loci\">27</a>. In <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> the marks are the exceptional and bad loci of the link contractions; in <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> they also include the restrictions of the transforms of the original divisor $D$. Each induced end-base contraction $B\\to Q$ is of Fano type over $Q$. Its geometric generic fibre is integral and has a rationally connected smooth proper model. In <span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span> all marked prime divisors on a link model are transforms of primes on the chosen common start: the maps from that start do not extract. Higher starts with the mark rule of Lemma <a href=\"#bd:higher-start\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bd:higher-start\">24</a> are allowed.*\n\n*In both parts the families are independent of the start, its resolution, the birational map, the length of a path, or the K3 test collection.",

        }
```

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-irrationality-with-ordinary-k3-category**: Above an ineffective threshold, a very general cubic in each admissible Hassett divisor is irrational while its Kuznetsov component is equivalent to the ordinary derived category of a smooth projective K3 surface.
- **invariant-intrinsic-conic-candidate**: The intrinsic correction at a conic node agrees with every eligible embedded surface-field candidate, including a zero candidate. It vanishes at link ends whose common base has dimension at most one.
- **invariant-integral-chow-triviality-despite-irrationality**: The very general irrational cubics in the stated admissible range have universally trivial Chow groups of zero-cycles and an integral Chow decomposition of the diagonal.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-effective-explicit-rationality-decider**: Turn the main theorem into an executable rationality decision procedure for an explicitly supplied cubic. -> The theorem supplies neither a computable discriminant threshold nor a membership test for its very-general locus.
- **anti-use-categorical-equivalence-implies-rationality**: Classify a cubic as rational because its Kuznetsov component has an ordinary K3 derived-category realization. -> The main theorem asserts irrationality and that categorical realization for the same very general cubics.
- **anti-use-hilbert-square-implies-cubic-rationality**: Infer rationality of a cubic from birationality of its Fano variety of lines to a K3 Hilbert square. -> The Hilbert-square subfamily contains very general irrational cubics above the threshold.
- **anti-use-chow-triviality-as-rationality-certificate**: Certify rationality from universal triviality of zero-cycles or an integral decomposition of the diagonal. -> These properties coexist with irrationality in the source examples.
- **anti-use-stable-rationality-conclusion**: Promote the ordinary irrationality conclusion to a statement about stable rationality. -> The source expressly leaves stable rationality unasserted.
- **anti-use-local-correction-as-intrinsic-before-comparison**: Assign a conic node its local generic-surface correction without comparing different embedded surface presentations. -> The local formula retains the chosen surface field; its compatibility requires the later intrinsic-correction argument and uniform degree restriction.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main irrationality result concerns very general members of complex moduli divisors above an existential threshold. The source states: “The threshold $d_0$ is ineffective.” It also defines “very general” by exclusion of a countable union of proper closed algebraic subsets separately in each divisor. These hypotheses do not provide a finite constructive input domain or an effective decision procedure for individual cubics. Finite checks of auxiliary intersection calculations would not certify the main theorem, the uniform threshold, or membership in its very-general locus.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-ineffective-discriminant-threshold** (nonconstructive): The common discriminant threshold is ineffective. The existence theorem does not supply a numerical cutoff usable to classify an explicitly given cubic. Blocked use: ['Implement the main theorem as an effective rationality decision procedure.', 'Assert that a particular discriminant exceeds the threshold without independent justification.']
- **limit-very-general-locus** (hypothesis): Irrationality in an admissible divisor is asserted for its very general members. It does not extend to every member or provide an effective membership test for a supplied cubic. Blocked use: ['Conclude that every cubic in a sufficiently large admissible divisor is irrational.', 'Apply the very-general conclusion to an individual equation solely from its discriminant.']
- **limit-admissibility-is-required** (hypothesis): The main divisor theorem requires admissibility, including the stated congruence and prime-divisibility restrictions. Large discriminant alone does not establish its hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Apply the divisor theorem to a discriminant checked only for size.', 'Drop the prime-divisibility restrictions while retaining the categorical and irrationality conclusions.']
- **limit-special-rational-members** (counterexample_scope): The theorem leaves room for special rational members of Hassett divisors. The source also records divisors consisting entirely of rational cubics, so the threshold and genericity qualifications cannot be discarded. Blocked use: ['Infer irrationality for a special rational sublocus from the ambient divisor theorem.', 'Extend the asymptotic assertion to all admissible discriminants.']
- **limit-full-integral-lattice-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The cubic criterion requires the prescribed transcendental rank, integral determinant above the uniform threshold, and scalar rational Hodge endomorphisms. An associated K3 surface alone does not establish these criterion hypotheses for a particular cubic. Blocked use: ['Apply the lattice criterion using only an associated K3 surface.', 'Replace verification of the endomorphism and rank conditions by the discriminant label.']
- **limit-rational-isometry-does-not-preserve-degree-test** (formal_scope): Cancellation uses whole saturated integral polarized Hodge lattices. A rational Hodge isomorphism does not by itself retain the integral discriminant needed to identify the exact polarization degree. Blocked use: ['Replace integral polarized Hodge-isometry classes by rational Hodge-isomorphism classes in the test collection.', 'Cancel only a rational part of a surface contribution while retaining the asserted integral degree.']
- **limit-k3-test-collection** (hypothesis): The test collection consists of Picard-number-one K3 surfaces with the fixed primitive ample degree and trivial automorphism group. The automorphism condition is essential to the small-orbit exclusions. Blocked use: ['Use the same orbit-vanishing rule for an unrestricted collection of K3 surfaces.', 'Count a field with a nonidentity complex automorphism as a test K3 field.']
- **limit-mrc-fibration-preconditions** (hypothesis): Preservation of the MRC test requires a geometrically integral generic fibre with a rationally connected smooth proper model. Dominance of a rational map alone is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Transfer the test across an arbitrary dominant rational map.', 'Ignore geometric integrality when comparing the MRC bases.']
- **limit-valuations-and-orbit-multiplicities** (formal_scope): The cocycle counts distinct divisorial valuations, while the finite étale test counts each residue-field factor once. Neither residue-field isomorphism nor extension degree supplies a substitute counting convention. Blocked use: ['Merge distinct prime valuations because their residue fields are isomorphic.', 'Multiply a tested orbit contribution by its field-extension degree.', 'Reverse the extraction-positive cocycle sign.']
- **limit-gromov-witten-tensor-regime** (hypothesis): The operator-algebra argument uses connected genus-zero invariants with rational diagonal fixed insertions and distinguished variable slots. Its reconstruction does not establish the same restrictions for arbitrary genera or arbitrary fixed Hodge types. Blocked use: ['Extend the scalar exceptional-block assertion to higher-genus invariants without proof.', 'Allow unrestricted off-diagonal fixed insertions in the reconstruction.']
- **limit-path-contraction-is-conditional** (hypothesis): The path contraction rule assumes that every oriented path vertex already satisfies its applicable tensor restriction. It does not establish those restrictions independently of the local lemma and reconstruction inductions. Blocked use: ['Use the path lemma to prove its own vertex hypotheses.', 'Contract arbitrary vertex tensors and infer vanishing mixed blocks.']
- **limit-bounded-marked-diagrams** (hypothesis): The uniform exclusions depend on bounded families carrying maps and marked bad loci. Bounded isomorphism classes of vertices alone do not supply the branch-location control used for residue covers. Blocked use: ['Apply the exclusions using only bounded underlying varieties.', 'Allow arbitrary maps between bounded vertices while assuming their marked diagrams remain bounded.']
- **limit-cover-degree-without-branch-control** (hypothesis): The bounded-cover exclusions require covers to be étale away from the marked boundary. A degree bound without control of branch locations does not satisfy the stated contract. Blocked use: ['Exclude large-degree K3 covering fields solely from a bounded extension degree.', 'Allow unrestricted new branch divisors while reusing the same genus bound.']
- **limit-order-of-uniform-choices** (formal_scope): The ordinary link diagrams first determine the ramification coefficient. The auxiliary log diagrams and terminalization bounds are then taken with that coefficient fixed, before choosing the final degree threshold. Blocked use: ['Choose a different coefficient independently at each link while claiming the same uniform bound.', 'Derive the coefficient from auxiliary families whose bounds already depend on that coefficient.']
- **limit-terminalization-not-arbitrary-resolution** (hypothesis): The terminalization theorem bounds models extracting only the specified low-discrepancy valuations and their subsequent non-extracting moves. It does not bound an arbitrary smooth resolution of the conic base. Blocked use: ['Treat all higher resolutions of a bounded base as a bounded family.', 'Allow surviving extracted valuations outside the stated discrepancy cutoff.']
- **limit-original-field-picard-ranks** (formal_scope): The generic-surface ranks are computed over the original function field. Neither the generic surfaces nor their genus-zero curve bases are assumed split, and geometric base change may increase Picard rank. Blocked use: ['Assume a rational point on the generic conic base.', 'Replace original-field Picard ranks by geometric Picard ranks in the two-ray reduction.']
- **limit-finite-pencil-enumeration-scope** (formal_scope): The finite pencil enumeration concerns the specified del Pezzo degrees in a supplied geometric plane blowup basis. Its cardinalities do not by themselves identify Galois orbit fields or certify the global irrationality theorem. Blocked use: ['Extend the displayed pencil list to arbitrary del Pezzo degrees.', 'Recover tested residue fields from cardinalities or rational permutation representations alone.', 'Promote a finite intersection-class check into verification of the uniform irrationality theorem.']
- **limit-nontrivial-two-ray-discriminant-bound** (edge_case): The singular-fibre bound belongs to the nontrivial generic two-ray case. A structured-isomorphism link has vanishing residual without any such discriminant-size bound. Blocked use: ['Impose the nontrivial-move bound on every conic structure.', 'Treat distinct conic structures on the same surface as the structured-isomorphism case automatically.']
- **limit-eligible-embedded-fields** (hypothesis): Eligibility requires a specified embedded surface field, a geometrically integral genus-zero presentation, and the original visible ramification bound. Abstractly isomorphic surface fields cannot be substituted for equal embedded subfields. Blocked use: ['Replace embedded-field equality by abstract field isomorphism in the uniqueness argument.', 'Apply intrinsicity to a presentation that violates the eligibility ramification bound.']
- **limit-retain-original-ramification-after-base-change** (formal_scope): Ramification support and residue covers are formed before extending constants and retained as marked data afterward. Recomputing the support from the extended Brauer class can erase divisors required by the boundedness argument. Blocked use: ['Delete an originally ramified divisor merely because its residue vanishes after extending constants.', 'Recompute eligibility from geometric ramification after extending the ground field.']
- **limit-local-correction-before-intrinsicity** (formal_scope): The local surface correction retains the chosen surface field. Its promotion to a function of the whole conic node requires the uniform large-degree intrinsicity theorem and agreement with every eligible candidate, including zero candidates. Blocked use: ['Telescope presentation-dependent corrections before proving intrinsicity.', 'Discard a zero candidate in favour of a nonzero candidate from another eligible field.']
- **limit-mori-point-vanishing-endpoints** (hypothesis): The cocycle vanishing theorem concerns the stated terminal rationally connected Mori fourfolds over points. General Mori nodes instead carry endpoint corrections, so their individual link cocycles need not vanish. Blocked use: ['Assume that the same polarized surface realizes every K3 association of the cubic.', 'Transfer extra properties between the categorical and Hilbert-square surfaces without an identification.']
- **limit-chow-triviality-does-not-detect-these-examples** (counterexample_scope): The stated irrational cubics have universally trivial zero-cycles and an integral Chow decomposition of the diagonal. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every sufficiently large admissible Hassett discriminant, we prove that a very general cubic fourfold of that discriminant is irrational, although its Kuznetsov component is equivalent to the ordinary derived category of a projective K3 surface. The discriminant threshold is ineffective. This disproves Kuznetsov's rationality conjecture. The same cubics have associated untwisted polarized K3 surfaces in the Hodge-theoretic sense, so they also disprove the sufficiency direction of the associated-K3 rationality prediction.

| [stmt-cub-generic](#stmt-cub-generic) | lemma | `cub:generic` | 51 | For each admissible $d$, a very general point $X\in\mathcal C_d$ satisfies |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A smooth cubic fourfold $X\subset\mathbf P^5_{\mathbb C}$ is rational if its field of rational functions is a purely transcendental extension of $\mathbb C$ of transcendence degree four. The connection between this birational question and K3 surfaces appears both in the middle cohomology and in the derived category. We prove that both connections can hold for the same irrational cubic.

The geometric connection grew out of explicit rationality constructions. Cubics containing quartic rational scrolls were studied by Fano (Fano 1943, 72) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Fano43](#ref-Fano43)]; Tregub gave constructions using two disjoint planes, quintic del Pezzo surfaces, and quartic scrolls (Tregub 1984, secs. 2–4) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Tregub84](#ref-Tregub84)]. Beauville and Donagi proved that the Fano variety $F(X)$, which parametrizes lines on $X$, is irreducible holomorphic symplectic. For sufficiently general Pfaffian cubics they identified $F(X)$ with $S^{[2]}$, the variety of length-two subschemes of a K3 surface $S$, and proved that the cubic is rational (Beauville and Donagi 1985, Propositions 1–2 and 5) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BeauvilleDonagi85](#ref-BeauvilleDonagi85)]. Hassett constructed further rational cubics containing a plane; in those parametrizations a family of exceptional lines is parametrized by a surface birational to a K3 surface (Hassett 1999, Theorem 4.2 and Proposition 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Hassett99](#ref-Hassett99)].

The Kuznetsov component of $X$ is the full triangulated subcategory $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ku}}(X)=\bigl\{F\in D^b(\operatorname{Coh}X):
 \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal O_X(i),F[k])=0
 \text{ for }0\le i\le2\text{ and }k\in\mathbb Z\bigr\}.$$ It gives the semiorthogonal decomposition $$D^b(\operatorname{Coh}X)
 =\langle\mathop{\mathrm{Ku}}(X),\mathcal O_X,\mathcal O_X(1),\mathcal O_X(2)\rangle.$$ Kuznetsov’s rationality conjecture asserts that $X$ is rational if and only if $\mathop{\mathrm{Ku}}(X)$ is equivalent to the derived category of a projective K3 surface (Kuznetsov 2010, Conjecture 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Kuznetsov10](#ref-Kuznetsov10)]. Here rationality means birationality over $\mathbb C$ with $\mathbf P^4$, and the K3 equivalence in this statement means an exact $\mathbb C$-linear equivalence with the ordinary category $D^b(\operatorname{Coh}S)$. The target has no Brauer twisting.

<a id="cub:admissible"></a>
Let $\mathcal C$ denote the moduli space of smooth complex cubic fourfolds. Write $h=c_1(\mathcal O_X(1))$. A *labelling of discriminant $d$* is a saturated positive-definite rank-two sublattice $$K\subset H^4(X,\mathbb Z)\cap H^{2,2}(X)$$ containing $h^2$, with $\det K=d$. The corresponding Hassett locus is denoted by $\mathcal C_d$. We call an integer $d$ *admissible* if $$\label{cub:admissible}
 d>6,\qquad d\equiv0,2\pmod6,\qquad
 4\nmid d,\quad 9\nmid d,\quad
 p\nmid d\ \text{for every odd prime }p\equiv2\pmod3.$$ Hassett’s period and lattice theorems make $\mathcal C_d$ a nonempty irreducible divisor for these discriminants and give its associated polarized K3 surfaces (Hassett 2000, Theorems 1.0.1 and 1.0.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Hassett00](#ref-Hassett00)]. Here an associated K3 surface has a primitive polarization $L$ of degree $d$ and an integral Hodge isometry $K^\perp\simeq L^\perp(-1)$, where the surface cup pairing is reversed.

Kuznetsov verified the categorical prediction for smooth Pfaffian cubics (Kuznetsov 2010, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Kuznetsov10](#ref-Kuznetsov10)]. Addington and Thomas proved that the Hodge-theoretic and categorical K3 associations agree on a dense open subset of each admissible divisor (Addington and Thomas 2014, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[AddingtonThomas14](#ref-AddingtonThomas14)]. The construction of stability conditions by Bayer, Lahoz, Macrì and Stellari (Bayer et al. 2023, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BLMS23](#ref-BLMS23)], together with the theory in families of Bayer, Lahoz, Macrì, Nuer, Perry and Stellari, gives an ordinary K3 derived category for every cubic having an associated K3 surface (Bayer et al. 2021, Corollary 29.7) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BLMPNS21](#ref-BLMPNS21)]. These results supply the categorical assertion below; the irrationality assertion is proved here.

Our main result disproves the categorical-to-rational implication of Kuznetsov’s conjecture on every sufficiently large admissible divisor.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Irrationality on large admissible Hassett divisors). *There is an integer $d_0$ such that, for every admissible $d>d_0$, a very general cubic fourfold $X$ in $\mathcal C_d$ is irrational and admits an exact $\mathbb C$-linear equivalence $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ku}}(X)\simeq D^b(\operatorname{Coh}S)$$ for a smooth projective K3 surface $S$.*

</div>

The threshold $d_0$ is ineffective. Some Hassett divisors consist entirely of rational cubics: Russo and Staglianò proved rationality throughout $\mathcal C_{26}$ and $\mathcal C_{38}$ using congruences of five-secant conics (Russo and Staglianò 2019, Theorems 4 and 7) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[RussoStagliano19](#ref-RussoStagliano19)], and throughout $\mathcal C_{42}$ using trisecant flops (Russo and Staglianò 2023, Theorem 5.12) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[RussoStagliano23](#ref-RussoStagliano23)]. Here “very general” means outside a countable union of proper closed algebraic subsets separately in each divisor $\mathcal C_d$. This qualification matters: Yang and Yu construct rational subloci of codimension two in the full moduli space inside every Hassett divisor (Yang and Yu 2020, Theorem 9) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[YangYu20](#ref-YangYu20)].

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The two evaluations of one divisorial invariant

Write $T_X$ for the integral lattice orthogonal to the integral Hodge classes in $H^4(X,\mathbb Z)$, with its cup-product pairing, and put $d=|\det T_X|$. The irrationality argument concerns cubics with $\operatorname{rank}T_X=21$, $d>2$, and $\operatorname{End}_{\mathrm{Hdg}}(T_X\otimes\mathbb Q)=\mathbb Q$. Suppose such an $X$ were rational, and choose a birational map $f:\mathbf P^4\dashrightarrow X$. We use one fixed collection $\mathcal S$ of Picard-number-one K3 surfaces: those whose entire integral transcendental Hodge lattice, after shifting Hodge types by $(1,1)$ and reversing the pairing, is isometric to $T_X$. The lattice hypotheses imply that these surfaces have primitive ample degree $d$ and trivial automorphism group (Lemma <a href="#gw:k3-center" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gw:k3-center">18</a>). We do not assume that this collection is nonempty: the hypothetical birational map will force a member of it.

The maximal rationally connected (MRC) quotient of a smooth projective variety is the base of its birationally determined rational fibration with rationally connected general fibres and non-uniruled base. For an integral variety $E$, let $u(E)$ be one if the MRC quotient of a smooth projective model of its function field is birational to a member of $\mathcal S$, and zero otherwise. Section <a href="#pre:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:section">2</a> defines $c_u(f)$ by counting the prime divisors acquired by the target with positive sign and those lost from the source with negative sign, each weighted by $u$. The count is finite and additive under composition. We evaluate this same invariant in two ways.

Smooth weak factorization expresses $f$ as blowups and blowdowns along smooth centers. Only surface centers can have nonzero test. Classically, a surface blowup adds its whole transcendental lattice, shifted to weight four with reversed pairing, as an integral orthogonal summand. The difficulty is to recognize that same summand on blowdown. Section <a href="#gw:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gw:section">3</a> uses genus-zero Gromov–Witten pairings with two variable transcendental inputs and all remaining insertions rational of diagonal Hodge type. For an intermediate fourfold $M$, define $T_M$ as for $X$. Each such pairing $B(a,b)$ determines an operator $A_B$ by $B(a,b)=q_M(A_Ba,b)$, where $q_M$ is the cup form on $T_M\otimes\mathbb Q$. The algebra generated by these operators has a separate scalar factor on each whole surface contribution. Its projectors force a later blowdown to remove a whole block. Intersecting that rational block with the ambient integral lattice recovers its entire integral lattice, so cancellation preserves the exact test class. Every nonzero block has a nonzero $(3,1)$ part, so $h^{3,1}(X)=1$ leaves one block in the final transcendental lattice. Rank $21$ makes its surface center birational to a Picard-number-one K3 surface, and its discriminant fixes the degree $d$. Thus one more member of the class $\mathcal S$ is added than removed, and Proposition <a href="#gw:net-center" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gw:net-center">19</a> gives $c_u(f)=1$.

The Sarkisov factorization passes through Mori fibre spaces: contractions $V\to B$ with terminal $\mathbb Q$-factorial projective fourfold $V$, $\dim B<4$, relative Picard number one, and relatively ample $-K_V$. These are the nodes of the factorization. An elementary birational transformation between nodes is a link; its endpoint bases map to a common base $Q$. The bases can change along the path. Sections <a href="#bd:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bd:section">4</a>–<a href="#sf:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sf:section">6</a> construct integer-valued functions $v,s$ on the nodes and prove, for sufficiently large $d$, $$c_u(\text{link})=(v+s)(V'/B')-(v+s)(V/B).$$ The value $v(V/B)$ is the finite cancelled difference between the tests of vertical divisors on $V$ and divisors on $B$. The value $s$ comes from the remaining generic-surface calculation.

At a conic-bundle node, the base field $L=\mathbb C(B)$ has transcendence degree three. For a surface subfield $k\subset L$ arising from a link, $L$ is the function field of a genus-zero curve over $k$. The surface calculation then gives a candidate for $s$, using the test on finite residue fields and covers determined by the conic’s Brauer ramification. The crucial point is that the candidates agree for every such presentation with the ramification bound supplied by the links. A second Sarkisov factorization, now on the threefold bases, compares these presentations. Bounded diagrams with their branch loci recorded give genus bounds incompatible with pencils on Picard-number-one K3 surfaces of sufficiently large degree. This proves independence with one bound for all test subcollections and factorizations. At nodes over a point both corrections vanish; the displayed differences therefore telescope to $c_u(f)=0$ by Theorem <a href="#sf:vanishing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sf:vanishing">49</a>.

The contradiction proves the irrationality criterion with one threshold for all the lattice data in its statement. Section <a href="#cub:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cub:section">7</a> then applies the standard period and categorical results: for each admissible $d$, a very general point of $\mathcal C_d$ has the required lattice data and an exact $\mathbb C$-linear equivalence of its Kuznetsov component with the ordinary derived category of a smooth projective K3 surface. The criterion therefore applies in every admissible divisor above the same threshold. Figure <a href="#fig:two-evaluations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:two-evaluations">1</a> summarizes the contradictory evaluations of the hypothetical map.

<figure id="fig:two-evaluations">

<figcaption>Assuming rationality gives a birational map $f : P^{4}⇢X$. Its two evaluations use the same collection of K3 surfaces. The right-hand evaluation holds above a degree threshold independent of the map, the test subcollection, and the number of links.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-two-evaluations"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:two-evaluations`)

Visual witness description of PDF page 4 (generated from the PDF page, not source text):

```text
A bifurcation diagram with top node $\mathbf{P}^4 \dashrightarrow X$ branching into two downward paths:
- Left path: an arrow pointing down-left labeled "smooth factorization" pointing to the text "whole integral surface blocks", followed by a vertical line, the label "Proposition 3.10", and a downward arrow pointing to "$c_u(f) = 1$".
- Right path: an arrow pointing down-right labeled "Mori factorization" pointing to the text "endpoint corrections $v + s$", followed by a vertical line, the label "Theorem 6.6", and a downward arrow pointing to "$c_u(f) = 0$".
```

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Consequences for K3 geometry and zero-cycles

The modern associated-K3 rationality prediction, in the explicit formulation recorded by Huybrechts (Huybrechts 2025, Conjecture 2.1, equation (2.1)) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[HuybrechtsUpdate25](#ref-HuybrechtsUpdate25)], asserts that a smooth complex cubic fourfold is rational if and only if the primitive cohomology of some polarized K3 surface admits a primitive isometric Hodge embedding into its primitive fourth cohomology, with the Tate twist and pairing convention used below.

<a id="stmt-cor-associated-k3"></a>
<div id="cor:associated-k3" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Failure of associated-K3 sufficiency). *Let $d_0$ be the threshold in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For every admissible $d>d_0$, a very general $X\in\mathcal C_d$ is irrational and admits a labelling $K$ of discriminant $d$ and a primitively polarized smooth projective K3 surface $(S_{\mathrm{Hdg}},L)$, with $L^2=d$, for which there is an integral Hodge isometry $$K^\perp \simeq L^\perp(-1).$$ Here $L^\perp\subset H^2(S_{\mathrm{Hdg}},\mathbb Z)$, and the surface cup pairing is reversed. Thus an associated untwisted polarized K3 surface in this Hodge-theoretic sense is not sufficient for rationality.*

</div>

The proof is given in Section <a href="#cub:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cub:consequences">7.3</a>.

<a id="stmt-cor-hilbert-square"></a>
<div id="cor:hilbert-square" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Failure of Hilbert-square sufficiency). *Let $d_0$ be the threshold in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For every integer $n\ge2$ such that $d=2n^2+2n+2>d_0$, a very general $X\in\mathcal C_d$ is irrational and its Fano variety of lines $F(X)$ is birational to the Hilbert square $S_{\mathrm{Hilb}}^{[2]}$ of a smooth projective K3 surface $S_{\mathrm{Hilb}}$. There are infinitely many such discriminants. Thus birationality of $F(X)$ to a K3 Hilbert square is not sufficient for rationality of $X$.*

</div>

The proof is given in Section <a href="#cub:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cub:consequences">7.3</a>.

The categorical surface $S$, the Hodge-associated surface $S_{\mathrm{Hdg}}$, and, in the Hilbert-square subfamily, the surface $S_{\mathrm{Hilb}}$ need not be identified. They witness different structures on the same cubic.

<a id="stmt-cor-chow-diagonal"></a>
<div id="cor:chow-diagonal" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4** (Integral Chow decompositions for the irrational cubics). *Let $d_0$ be the threshold in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. For every admissible $d>d_0$, a very general $X\in\mathcal C_d$ is irrational and has universally trivial $\operatorname{CH}_0$: for every field extension $F/\mathbb C$, the degree map $$\deg\colon\operatorname{CH}_0(X_F)\longrightarrow\mathbb Z$$ is an isomorphism. This universal $\operatorname{CH}_0$ property is equivalent to the existence of a point $x\in X(\mathbb C)$, a proper closed algebraic subset $D\subsetneq X$, and a codimension-four cycle $\Gamma$ with integer coefficients supported on $D\times X$ such that $$[\Delta_X]=[X\times\{x\}]+[\Gamma]
 \quad\text{in }\operatorname{CH}^4(X\times X)$$ with integral coefficients.*

</div>

The proof is given in Section <a href="#cub:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cub:consequences">7.3</a>.

This applies in particular to the associated-K3 examples in Corollary <a href="#cor:associated-k3" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:associated-k3">2</a> and the Hilbert-square subfamily in Corollary <a href="#cor:hilbert-square" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:hilbert-square">3</a>. The asserted identity is an integral Chow-theoretic decomposition, not merely a rational cohomological decomposition; thus the decomposition-of-the-diagonal obstruction does not detect the irrationality of these examples. A variety is stably rational if its product with some projective space is rational. Stable rationality implies universal $\operatorname{CH}_0$-triviality (Voisin 2017) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Voisin17](#ref-Voisin17)]; the corollary supplies this necessary condition for the irrational cubics. No assertion about stable rationality is made.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Relation to birational invariants and quantum methods

The existence of an associated K3 is distinct from the stronger condition that the Fano variety of lines be birational to a Hilbert square of a K3 surface (Addington 2016, Theorems 1 and 2) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Addington16](#ref-Addington16)]. A related motivic approach uses the identity of Galkin and Shinder between a cubic, its Hilbert square and its Fano variety of lines in the Grothendieck ring (Galkin and Shinder 2014, Theorem 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[GalkinShinder14](#ref-GalkinShinder14)]. Their rationality consequence assumes a cancellation conjecture for the class of the affine line (Galkin and Shinder 2014, Conjecture 2.7 and Theorem 7.5) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[GalkinShinder14](#ref-GalkinShinder14)]; Borisov proved that this global cancellation conjecture fails (Borisov 2018, Theorem 2.12) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Borisov18](#ref-Borisov18)]. Our argument uses an additive invariant of individual birational maps.

Quantum cohomology and Hodge theory provide another approach to irrationality. Iritani’s blowup theorem decomposes the quantum $D$-module after a formal extension of coefficients and change of variables (Iritani 2025, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Iritani25](#ref-Iritani25)]. Using this quantum blowup behavior, Katzarkov, Kontsevich, Pantev and Yu construct Hodge atoms and obtain an irrationality theorem for a very general cubic in the full moduli space (Katzarkov et al. 2026, Theorem 6.8) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[KKPY26](#ref-KKPY26)]. Their very-general hypothesis does not cover a prescribed Hassett divisor. Guéré obtains a rational Hodge-theoretic constraint on a rational cubic (Guéré 2026, Theorem 63) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Guere26](#ref-Guere26)]; that conclusion does not assert an integral isometry of the intersection lattices. The argument here retains whole integral polarized transcendental lattices through cancellation. Their integral discriminants determine the exact degrees of the K3 surfaces in the fixed test collection. The integral comparison and the uniform bound for the relevant surface contributions are proved in Sections <a href="#gw:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gw:section">3</a>–<a href="#sf:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sf:section">6</a>.

Integral surface-transcendental lattices also enter Kulikov’s approach, which derives irrationality of a very general cubic fourfold from an indecomposability conjecture for those lattices (Kulikov 2008, Proposition 1) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Kulikov08](#ref-Kulikov08)]. Auel, Böhning and Graf von Bothmer disproved that conjecture for the sextic Fermat surface (Auel, Böhning, and Graf von Bothmer 2013, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[AuelBohningBothmer13](#ref-AuelBohningBothmer13)]. Here the independent factors of the operator algebra distinguish whole surface contributions even when their transcendental Hodge structures are reducible; no general indecomposability assertion is used.

Fay proves that a very general member of a special divisor is irrational when the quaternion class $(d/2,-3)$ over $\mathbb Q$ is nonzero (Fay 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Fay26](#ref-Fay26)]. The class vanishes exactly when the valuation of $d/2$ is even at every prime congruent to two modulo three (Fay 2026, condition $(**')$) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Fay26](#ref-Fay26)]. For every admissible $d$, all these valuations are zero: $4\nmid d$ makes $d/2$ odd, and admissibility excludes the other such primes. Thus Fay’s quaternion obstruction vanishes throughout the range of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

The divisorial invariant is a specialization of the motivic invariants of birational maps studied by Lin and Shinder (Lin and Shinder 2024) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[LinShinder24](#ref-LinShinder24)]. Their later work develops horizontal and vertical versions (Lin and Shinder 2026, Definition 2.3 and Lemmas 2.4–2.5) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[LinShinder26](#ref-LinShinder26)] and constructs unbounded contributions from elliptic surfaces (Lin and Shinder 2026, Theorem 1.2 and Section 4) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[LinShinder26](#ref-LinShinder26)]. The surface calculation adapts the virtual Néron–Severi viewpoint of Lin, Shinder and Zimmermann (Lin, Shinder, and Zimmermann 2023, Definition 5.2 and Proposition 5.5) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[LinShinderZimmermann23](#ref-LinShinderZimmermann23)]. Our uniform exclusion concerns Picard-number-one K3 surfaces of large degree and remains uniform when their integral transcendental Hodge-isometry class is prescribed.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Notation and organization

All varieties and function fields are over $\mathbb C$, except for the explicit generic-field arguments. Cohomology coefficients are specified where used; transcendental lattices always mean integral lattices modulo torsion. For reference, the main notation is collected below.

<div class="center">

| Symbol             | Meaning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
|:-------------------|:----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| $T_M,\ q_M$        | Integral transcendental middle lattice of a fourfold and its cup form.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| $T_Z(-1)$          | Surface transcendental lattice shifted to weight four, with pairing $-q_Z$.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| $G_M$              | Rational operator algebra on $T_M\otimes\mathbb Q$, defined from genus-zero pairings in Definition <a href="#gw:algebra" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gw:algebra">11</a>.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| $\mathcal S,\ u$   | A degree-$d$ K3 test collection and its MRC indicator.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| $c_u(f)$           | Target-acquired minus source-lost prime divisors, weighted by $u$.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| $V/B,\ Q$          | A Mori fibre-space node and the common base of a link.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| $v,\ h_Q$          | Integer vertical-divisor correction and its portion dominating $Q$; <a href="#sl:v-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sl:v-definition">[sl:v-definition]</a> and <a href="#sl:h-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sl:h-definition">[sl:h-definition]</a>.                                                                                                                              |
| $w,\ \sigma_k,\ s$ | Integer corrections on a generic surface, a conic with a chosen surface subfield, and an entire Mori node; Definition <a href="#sl:w-def" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sl:w-def">40</a>, <a href="#sf:candidate" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sf:candidate">[sf:candidate]</a>, and <a href="#sf:node-function" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sf:node-function">[sf:node-function]</a>. |

</div>

Section <a href="#pre:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:section">2</a> defines the test and the divisorial cocycle. Section <a href="#gw:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gw:section">3</a> proves the whole-lattice calculation in smooth factorizations. Section <a href="#bd:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bd:section">4</a> establishes the bounded marked diagrams and fixes the order of the uniform choices. Section <a href="#sl:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sl:section">5</a> computes the correction on generic surfaces; Section <a href="#sf:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sf:section">6</a> makes it intrinsic and proves vanishing by telescoping. Section <a href="#cub:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cub:section">7</a> applies the resulting irrationality criterion to every sufficiently large admissible Hassett divisor and proves the three corollaries stated above.

<a id="pre:section"></a>
### Birational fields and a divisorial cocycle

We define the field test and the additive birational invariant used in both factorizations. On a smooth blowup the invariant will be the test of the center, with a sign determined by the direction of the map.

All varieties and maps are over $\mathbb C$, unless a function field is explicitly specified. A variety is integral. When a finite union is used, every sum is taken over its integral components. A model of a finitely generated field $F/\mathbb C$ is a variety with function field $F$; a smooth projective model may always be chosen. A fourfold is *rational* if it is birational over $\mathbb C$ to $\mathbb P^4$. Rational connectedness of a field means rational connectedness of a smooth projective model. We use characteristic-zero resolution and principalization in the projective category, originating with Hironaka (Hironaka 1964) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Hironaka64](#ref-Hironaka64)]; precise forms are given in (Włodarczyk 2005, Theorems 1.0.1–1.0.3) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Wlodarczyk05](#ref-Wlodarczyk05)]. Resolving the closure of a joint graph gives a common smooth projective resolution of finitely many rational maps. Principalization makes the marked ideals and exceptional boundary into a simple normal-crossing support; see also (Abramovich et al. 2002, secs. 1.2.2–1.2.3) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[AKMW02](#ref-AKMW02)].

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The K3 test

For a smooth projective variety $V$, write $V\dashrightarrow R(V)$ for its maximal rationally connected quotient. Its general fibres are rationally connected, its base is not uniruled, and the base is determined up to birational equivalence. We use the standard quotient theorem and the theorem that a fibration with rationally connected base and general fibre has rationally connected total space; see (Kollár 1996, vol. 32, chap. IV, §5) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[Kollar96](#ref-Kollar96)] and (Graber, Harris, and Starr 2003, Corollaries 1.3–1.4) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[GHS03](#ref-GHS03)]. The quotient has a proper morphism representative on suitable dense open subsets (Campana 1992, sec. 0.7 and Theorem 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[Campana92](#ref-Campana92)].

Fix a positive integer $d>2$ and any collection $\mathcal S$ of isomorphism classes of smooth projective K3 surfaces $S$ such that <a id="pre:test-collection"></a>$$\label{pre:test-collection}
 \operatorname{Pic}(S)=\mathbb ZL_S,\qquad L_S\text{ is ample},\qquad
 L_S^2=d,\qquad \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathbb C}(S)=\{1\}.$$ For odd $d$ the collection is empty. The collection can be restricted by any further condition, including an integral polarized transcendental Hodge-isometry condition. Every degree threshold proved below is uniform in this choice of subcollection.

<a id="stmt-pre-test"></a>
<div id="pre:test" class="definition">

**Definition 5** (The test function). For a finitely generated field $F/\mathbb C$, choose a smooth projective model $V$ and set $$u(F)=
 \begin{cases}
  1,&\text{if }R(V)\text{ is birational to some }S\in\mathcal S,\\
  0,&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}$$ For an integral variety $V$, put $u(V)=u(\mathbb C(V))$.

</div>

Birational invariance and uniqueness of the quotient make this definition independent of the model. A K3 surface is not uniruled. Moreover, two birational smooth projective K3 surfaces are isomorphic. One way to see the latter assertion is to resolve a birational map as $S\xleftarrow{p}W\xrightarrow{q}S'$. The pullbacks of the nowhere vanishing two-forms on $S$ and $S'$ span the same one-dimensional space $H^0(W,\Omega_W^2)$. Their zero divisors therefore have the same support. These supports are respectively the exceptional loci of $p$ and $q$. Every exceptional fibre is connected, so $q$ contracts every fibre of $p$, and conversely. The image of $(p,q)$ in $S\times S'$ projects properly, quasi-finitely and birationally to the normal surface $S$, so that projection is an isomorphism. Descending in both directions gives inverse morphisms. In particular, $\operatorname{Bir}_{\mathbb C}(S)=\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathbb C}(S)$ for a K3 surface.

<a id="stmt-pre-mrc-fibration"></a>
<div id="pre:mrc-fibration" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (Rationally connected fibrations). *Let $V\dashrightarrow W$ be a dominant rational map whose geometric generic fibre is integral and has a rationally connected smooth proper model. Then $R(V)$ and $R(W)$ are birational, and hence $u(V)=u(W)$. In particular, for any vector bundle $\mathcal E$ of positive rank on an integral variety $W$, $$u(\mathbb P_W(\mathcal E))=u(W).$$ The test vanishes on rationally connected varieties and on varieties of dimension at most one. For an integral surface $W$, it is one precisely when $W$ is birational to a member of $\mathcal S$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-mrc-fibration"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-mrc-fibration</summary>

*Proof.* Resolve the rational maps and use smooth projective models. Compose $V\dashrightarrow W$ with the maximal rationally connected quotient of $W$. A general fibre of the composite fibres over a rationally connected general fibre of $W\dashrightarrow R(W)$, with rationally connected general fibre. It is rationally connected by the fibration theorem of Graber–Harris–Starr (Graber, Harris, and Starr 2003, Corollary 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[GHS03](#ref-GHS03)]. Since $R(W)$ is not uniruled, this composite is a maximal rationally connected quotient of $V$. Indeed, a family of rational curves through general points of $V$ that is not vertical would induce a covering family of rational curves on $R(W)$. Uniqueness of the quotient proves the first assertion. A projective bundle has a projective-space generic fibre. The remaining statements follow from the dimension of the quotient, and from the fact that a non-uniruled surface is its own quotient. ◻

</details>

<a id="pre:finite-orbits"></a>
We also use $u$ on finite field extensions and finite Galois sets. If $k/\mathbb C$ is finitely generated and a finite étale $k$-algebra is $A=\prod_i k_i$, define $$\label{pre:finite-orbits}
 u(A)=\sum_i u(k_i).$$ Equivalently, for a finite set with continuous action of $\operatorname{Gal}(\bar k/k)$, sum once over its Galois orbits, using the finite residue field belonging to each orbit. There is no factor $[k_i:k]$ in this sum. Each $k_i$ is viewed as a field over $\mathbb C$. Geometric generic fibres over algebraic closures of function fields will be used to obtain bounds, while $u$ continues to be evaluated on the original finitely generated fields over $\mathbb C$.

<a id="stmt-pre-small-orbits"></a>
<div id="pre:small-orbits" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Small orbits). *Suppose $k\simeq\mathbb C(x,y)$. A finite Galois orbit of size one or two has test value zero. More generally, if a finite extension $\ell/k$ has a nonidentity $\mathbb C$-automorphism, then $u(\ell)=0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-small-orbits"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-small-orbits</summary>

*Proof.* The field $k$ is rational. If $u(\ell)=1$, the surface field $\ell$ is the function field of a member of $\mathcal S$, by Lemma <a href="#pre:mrc-fibration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:mrc-fibration">6</a>. A nonidentity field automorphism would give a nonidentity birational, hence regular, automorphism of that K3 surface. This contradicts the choice of $\mathcal S$. A quadratic extension in characteristic zero has its nonidentity involution. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Prime divisors as valuations

Let $X$ be a normal projective variety with function field $F$. A prime divisor $E\subset X$ determines the normalized discrete valuation $\operatorname{ord}_E:F^*\to\mathbb Z$, whose residue field is $\mathbb C(E)$. Write $\mathcal D_F(X)$ for this set of valuations, using a specified identification $\mathbb C(X)\simeq F$ when necessary. Distinct valuations are counted separately even when their residue fields are isomorphic.

<a id="pre:cocycle-definition"></a>
For a birational map $f:X\dashrightarrow Y$, identify the function fields by $f^*: \mathbb C(Y)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb C(X)=F$ and define $$\label{pre:cocycle-definition}
 c_u(f)=
 \sum_{\nu\in\mathcal D_F(Y)\setminus\mathcal D_F(X)}u(\kappa(\nu))
 -\sum_{\nu\in\mathcal D_F(X)\setminus\mathcal D_F(Y)}u(\kappa(\nu)).$$ Thus divisors acquired by the target have positive sign. The target’s valuation set is transported using $f^*$ even when $X=Y$. This is the divisorial invariant of Lin and Shinder, composed with the MRC/K3 test $u$; the sign agrees with their target-acquired minus source-lost convention (Lin and Shinder 2024, Definition 2.1, Lemma 2.2 and Proposition 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[LinShinder24](#ref-LinShinder24)].

<a id="stmt-pre-cocycle"></a>
<div id="pre:cocycle" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Divisorial cocycle). *The sums in <a href="#pre:cocycle-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:cocycle-definition">[pre:cocycle-definition]</a> are finite. For birational maps $f:X\dashrightarrow Y$ and $g:Y\dashrightarrow Z$ of normal projective varieties, <a id="pre:cocycle-additivity"></a>$$\label{pre:cocycle-additivity}
 c_u(g\circ f)=c_u(f)+c_u(g),\qquad
 c_u(f^{-1})=-c_u(f).$$ An isomorphism in codimension one has value zero. If $\pi:\widetilde X\to X$ is the blow-up of a smooth projective variety along a smooth center with connected components $Z_i$ of codimension at least two, then <a id="pre:blowup-sign"></a>$$\label{pre:blowup-sign}
 c_u(\pi^{-1})=\sum_i u(Z_i),\qquad
 c_u(\pi)=-\sum_i u(Z_i).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-pre-cocycle"><summary>Proof of stmt-pre-cocycle</summary>

*Proof.* Choose dense open subsets on which $f$ is an isomorphism. Every divisor whose generic point belongs to these opens has a corresponding divisor on the other model with exactly the same valuation. Any valuation in the symmetric difference must therefore be represented by a divisorial component of one of the two closed complements. There are finitely many such components. This also proves that an isomorphism in codimension one has value zero.

For the composition use the common field $F=\mathbb C(X)$, transporting $\mathbb C(Y)$ by $f^*$ and $\mathbb C(Z)$ by $f^*g^*$. Put $D_X=\mathcal D_F(X)$, $D_Y=\mathcal D_F(Y)$ and $D_Z=\mathcal D_F(Z)$. Their pairwise symmetric differences are finite. On the set of all prime divisorial valuations of $F$, $$\mathbf 1_{D_Z}-\mathbf 1_{D_X}
 = (\mathbf 1_{D_Y}-\mathbf 1_{D_X})
   +(\mathbf 1_{D_Z}-\mathbf 1_{D_Y}).$$ All three differences have finite support. Multiplying by $u(\kappa(\nu))$ and summing proves additivity. Transport by a $\mathbb C$-field isomorphism identifies residue fields, so the last term is precisely $c_u(g)$. Reversing the two valuation sets gives the inverse formula.

For the blow-up, the target of the extraction $\pi^{-1}:X\dashrightarrow\widetilde X$ retains every original divisorial valuation and adds exactly one exceptional divisor $E_i=\mathbb P_{Z_i}(N_{Z_i/X})$ for each $Z_i$. Its residue field is purely transcendental over $\mathbb C(Z_i)$, of transcendence degree $\operatorname{codim}_X(Z_i)-1$. Lemma <a href="#pre:mrc-fibration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:mrc-fibration">6</a> gives $u(E_i)=u(Z_i)$. The stated signs now follow from the definition and the inverse formula. ◻

</details>

For the generic-surface interpretation, let $V\to Q$ have a smooth surface generic fibre over $k=\mathbb C(Q)$. Blowing up a closed point $P$ of that fibre creates the exceptional curve $\mathbb P^1_{\kappa(P)}$. Its function field is $\kappa(P)(t)$, so the corresponding divisorial valuation of $\mathbb C(V)$ contributes $u(\kappa(P))$ once, by Lemma <a href="#pre:mrc-fibration" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:mrc-fibration">6</a>. There is no factor $[\kappa(P):k]$. This is the compatibility between <a href="#pre:finite-orbits" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="pre:finite-orbits">[pre:finite-orbits]</a> and the divisorial count used for generic surface links.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The smooth factorization used below

<a id="stmt-pre-weak-factorization"></a>
<div id="pre:weak-factorization" class="theorem">

**Theorem 9** (Weak factorization). *A birational map between smooth projective varieties over $\mathbb C$ factors into a finite zigzag of blow-ups and blow-downs along smooth connected centers, with all intermediate varieties smooth and projective. Nontrivial steps may be taken to have centers of codimension at least two.*

</div>

This is the projective case of (Abramovich et al. 2002, Theorem 0.1.1) <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[AKMW02](#ref-AKMW02)]; blowing up a smooth divisor is an isomorphism and may be omitted. In a factorization of a birational map from $\mathbb P^4$, every intermediate variety is itself a smooth projective rational fourfold, hence rationally connected. Its centers have dimension zero, one, or two. Lemma <a href="#pre:cocycle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pre:cocycle">8</a> computes $c_u$ as the signed sum of their tests along the zigzag; only surface centers can have nonzero test.

<a id="gw:section"></a>
### Whole transcendental lattices under factorization

We construct an operator algebra which distinguishes the whole transcendental contribution of each surface center. Its role is to make the classical integral blowup decomposition compatible with cancellation in an arbitrary weak factorization. Quantum blowup decompositions also appear in Iritani’s decomposition of quantum $D$-modules and quantum cohomology $F$-manifolds (Iritani 2025, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[Iritani25](#ref-Iritani25)]. That decomposition uses a formal Novikov extension and a change of variables. Here we study the rational operator algebra in the classical blowup splitting and its saturated integral transcendental summands.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Hodge structures and the operator algebra

For a smooth projective variety $Y$, write $$H^{2i}(Y,\mathbb Q)_{\mathrm{Hdg}}
 =H^{2i}(Y,\mathbb Q)\cap H^{i,i}(Y).$$ If $M$ is a rationally connected smooth projective fourfold, set $$L_M=H^4(M,\mathbb Z)/\mathrm{tors},\qquad
 T_M=L_M\cap H^4(M,\mathbb Q)_{\mathrm{Hdg}}^{\perp}.$$ The orthogonal is taken for the cup form $q_M$. For a smooth projective surface $Z$, use the analogous definition $$T_Z=(H^2(Z,\mathbb Z)/\mathrm{tors})\cap
              H^2(Z,\mathbb Q)_{\mathrm{Hdg}}^{\perp},$$ with surface cup form $q_Z$. We always divide out torsion before discussing integral lattices. When comparing with degree four, $T_Z(-1)$ means the Tate shift to weight four, equipped with the form $-q_Z$.

<a id="stmt-gw-hodge"></a>
<div id="gw:hodge" class="lemma">

**Lemma 10**. *The forms on $T_M$ and $T_Z$ are nondegenerate. The rational Hodge structure $T_{M,\mathbb Q}$ is generated by its $(3,1)$ and $(1,3)$ subspaces, and $T_{Z,\mathbb Q}$ is generated by its $(2,0)$ and $(0,2)$ subspaces. The only off-diagonal types in the even cohomology of $M$ are $(3,1)$ and $(1,3)$ in degree four. If $i:Z\hookrightarrow M$ is a surface embedding, then $$i^*T_{M,\mathbb Q}=0,\qquad i_*T_{Z,\mathbb Q}=0.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-gw-hodge"><summary>Proof of stmt-gw-hodge</summary>

*Proof.* Rational connectedness gives $H^{p,0}(M)=0$ for $p>0$ (Araujo and Kollár 2002, Theorem 30) <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[AraujoKollar02](#ref-AraujoKollar02)]. We use the Hodge decomposition, functoriality and Lefschetz polarizations in (Voisin 2016, secs. 2.1–2.2) <a id="pm-cite-42"></a>[[VoisinHodge2016](#ref-VoisinHodge2016)]; the Hodge-complement statement is Lemma 1 there. Thus $H^2(M,\mathbb Q)$ is of type $(1,1)$; hard Lefschetz gives the analogous statement in degree six. The remaining assertion about types follows from Hodge symmetry. In degree four all nonprimitive Lefschetz summands are rational Hodge classes. Hodge–Riemann therefore makes their orthogonal primitive transcendental part nondegenerate. The same argument in degree two applies to a surface, using an ample class to split off its nonprimitive part. Alternatively, the nondegeneracy on the Hodge-class space follows on each primitive Lefschetz summand from definiteness.

By semisimplicity of polarizable rational Hodge structures, the substructure generated by the stated off-diagonal types has a Hodge complement in the transcendental structure. That complement would have only diagonal type, and hence would consist of rational Hodge classes. It is zero by the definition and nondegeneracy of the transcendental part. Finally, $i^*$ takes the off-diagonal part of $H^4(M)$ into $H^4(Z)$, which has only type $(2,2)$; and $i_*$ takes the off-diagonal part of $H^2(Z)$ into $H^6(M)$, which has only type $(3,3)$. Both maps vanish on the generating types and therefore on the whole corresponding rational Hodge structure. ◻

</details>

All Gromov–Witten invariants below are connected, of genus zero, and use cohomology with rational coefficients. The *Hodge difference* of a class of type $(p,q)$ is $p-q$. All degrees are collected by numerical curve class: an invariant denotes the sum over homology classes with the specified numerical class. This sum is finite in every bounded ample degree, and all degree comparisons below use an integral ample class. Other than the two specified variable insertions, every insertion is a rational class of diagonal Hodge type. Descendant powers at all ordinary markings, including the two variable markings, are arbitrary nonnegative integers. These two markings carry distinct formal labels in every weighted list and degeneration graph. The labels remain distinct even when the two vectors agree or one is zero. Automorphism and gluing coefficients thus depend on the fixed discrete data, independently of the variable vectors.

<a id="stmt-gw-algebra"></a>
<div id="gw:algebra" class="definition">

**Definition 11**. For every invariant $$B(u,v)=
 \left\langle\tau_a(u),\tau_b(v),
       \tau_{a_1}(\gamma_1),\ldots,\tau_{a_n}(\gamma_n)
 \right\rangle^M_{0,\beta},\qquad u,v\in T_{M,\mathbb Q},$$ let $A_B\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathbb Q}(T_{M,\mathbb Q})$ be determined by $B(u,v)=q_M(A_Bu,v)$. Let $G_M$ be the unital rational algebra generated by all such operators. On the zero space we take the zero algebra.

</div>

The virtual classes, evaluation maps, and cotangent classes defining these invariants respect Hodge structures. Thus $A_B$ is a Hodge endomorphism. Interchanging the two variable markings supplies its adjoint. In particular, every expression in $G_M$ is a rational Hodge endomorphism. By Lemma <a href="#gw:hodge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="gw:hodge">10</a>, equality of two such expressions can be checked on $(3,1)$, by pairing their images with $(1,3)$. We will use the analogous test on surfaces, after the indicated Tate shift. Accordingly, when analyzing a tensor on copies of $T_M$ or $T_Z$, we first test its two inputs on opposite off-diagonal types and then use this Hodge-generation statement to recover the full rational tensor.

<a id="stmt-gw-blowup"></a>
